ecomOS Rooms
Draft for Kevin, 25 Sep 2026

Every part of the business as a screen a founder can act on alone.

A room shows one domain's numbers from the brand's own data, turns them into sentences with a cause and a size using rules in code, draws them, and puts the action button next to each one. The agent is optional: a founder or an agency person can read the room and decide. Screens are drawn in the "nothing connected yet" state, so every tile reads unavailable, the way the product will before data lands.

Across the stackTodayBrand brainExperiments and memoryReviews and reports
SiteSite and speedLanding pages and CROStore health
Creative and copyCreativeCopy
Media buyingPaid socialGoogle AdsAffiliate, marketplace and retail media
Lifecycle and customersEmail and messagingCustomers and CXOffers and pricing
Organic and searchOrganic and contentSEOAnswer engines

Money and opsFinance and cashMeasurement and data healthTeam and opsSecurity
BrandBrand and customersCompetitors and marketWild bets and PR

With nothing connected, the room shows unavailable for every stage and says which connector each one needs; the cross-brand list, WhatsApp row and same-day ticker simply do not render until those sources exist. The first thing to appear once the metric contract has 5 full days of history is the pipeline waterfall with the top break named; the top 10 cards follow once Propose has run once.

Questions this room answers

  • where is the business actually broken right now
    see which pipeline stage is losing the most money today · DIAG-C01, ORCH-C01
  • what is this worth in rupees this week
    know how much the break is costing before deciding to act · DIAG-C01
  • what should I approve, send out, or park
    act on the top 10 ideas for the break in under a minute each · PROP-C06, ORCH-C03
  • are we actually growing or just booking orders that will bounce back
    see gross orders next to what they are worth after RTO and COD confirmation · LOOP-C23
  • did anything blow past a limit today
    catch a mid-day number that has already crossed a line, before end of day · LOOP-C40
  • what needs my decision right now
    see what is waiting on me across every queue, not per person · ORCH-C02, REG-C08
  • will this new test mess up a read I am already waiting on
    catch a test that would contaminate one already running · LOOP-C35
  • which client fire do I put out first today
    for an agency, see which brand needs attention first across the whole book · LOOP-C21
  • what do I need to know walking into this call today
    get a short brief before a named meeting with a vendor, investor or platform rep · LOOP-C17
  • can I just reply to decide
    approve or park a card from my phone without opening the page · LOOP-C42, LOOP-C42
  • what is due before the next festival or sale window
    know the seasonal deadline that is closest and whether prep is on track · LOOP-C05
  • does this one review or one stock issue need my eyes now
    see a single-item escalation that is too small for crisis mode but too risky to sit in the queue · LOOP-C06
  • did we actually cause this, or did something else
    trust that a number moved because of something we did, not something external · LOOP-C02
  • what can't the system see right now
    see what is unreadable today and why, instead of assuming it is fine · DIAG-C02

How it is drawn

  • stage waterfall waterfall / funnel chart: each pipeline stage with the break marked and the money at stake labelled exists today
  • stake bar horizontal bar chart: money at stake per stage, ranked
  • top 10 card stack card list with inline buttons: the top 10 proposal cards with why-now, effect, effort, risk and 3 buttons each exists today
  • trend sparkline row small multiples of sparklines: each stage's last 5 windows, flagging the 2-window-running movers
  • cross-brand rank list ranked list, agency view only: every brand's top item on one scale of stake and urgency, brand-tagged
  • same-day alert ticker compact alert strip: triggers crossed today with time and metric

Not in the usual tools

  • money-at-stake per stage, computed in code from your own trailing history and a peer band, before any fix is proposed
    vs Shopify Analytics and Klaviyo dashboards show revenue, sessions and conversion rate, not a stage-level stake number with a peer band
  • gross orders shown net of expected RTO and split by COD versus prepaid before naming a bottleneck
    vs generic ecommerce dashboards report gross orders only; no packaged Shopify or Triple Whale view nets out India RTO by default
  • one ranked list across every brand an agency runs, by stake and urgency on the same scale
    vs AgencyAnalytics and Whatagraph give per-client reports and roll-ups, not a single cross-client priority queue
  • same-day alert on a pre-agreed hard trigger, separate from full crisis mode and from tomorrow's batch ranking
    vs most ecommerce dashboards check once a day; ads and analytics platforms alert on their own metrics only, not a founder-defined cross-channel trigger
  • approve, reject or park a specific card by a single WhatsApp reply, matched back to its card id
    vs WhatsApp business tools like Interakt and AiSensy handle customer-facing templates and support replies, not an internal founder decision channel tied to a proposal card
  • a test flagged before it starts if it shares an audience or metric window with one already running
    vs enterprise experimentation platforms like GrowthBook check this inside one engineering team's own test list, not across marketing ideas drawn from a bank, an audit and the web

With no connectors granted, every section shows unavailable with the connector it needs, and the freshness x confidence heatmap is blank except for anything pulled from the site crawl, which needs no connector grant. The first thing to appear is the claims tier bar once the site is crawled once; contract renewals and marketplace parity appear only after those specific data sources are added.

Questions this room answers

  • can I trust what this page is telling me right now
    see how fresh and how trustworthy each part of what the system knows about the brand is · BRN-C12
  • what can and can't we say about this product
    see which claims are approved, which are only site-backed, and which are forbidden · BRN-C03
  • why did we decide that, and when
    see every founder decision with its reason, dated, in one log · BRN-C08
  • what does the system need from me to get unstuck
    see open questions the system cannot answer on its own · BRN-C07
  • the document says one thing and the site says another, which wins
    see where two sources disagree before trusting either · BRN-C05
  • did a price, a policy or a claim change without me knowing
    see what changed on the site since the last check · BRN-C01
  • is any of our data actually stale right now
    see which connectors are working and which failed · BRN-C02, BRN-C14
  • what contract needs my attention before it auto-renews
    see vendor and platform contracts with renewal dates before they lapse · LOOP-C28
  • is this page legally compliant before it goes live
    check a page or invoice carries the disclosures India's rules require before it ships · LOOP-C27
  • does the agency staff see more of our brain than they should
    see who can see which part of the brand's data · LOOP-C29
  • is someone undercutting our price on Amazon or Flipkart
    see the brand's own marketplace listings against the D2C site · PROD-C46
  • if we part ways with this agency, can we take everything with us
    export the whole brain cleanly when a relationship ends · LOOP-C22

How it is drawn

  • freshness x confidence heatmap heatmap grid: each brain section on freshness (rows) against confidence tier (columns)
  • claims tier bar stacked bar: count of claims by tier: approved, site-backed, forbidden, untiered
  • decisions log timeline vertical timeline: founder decisions with reason and date, most recent first
  • conflict table two-column comparison table: each unresolved conflict, source A value versus source B value, with the site marked as current default
  • contract renewal calendar horizontal timeline / gantt strip: each vendor contract's renewal date with the alert window marked
  • permission matrix grid: brain sections (rows) against roles or people (columns) with view rights marked
  • marketplace parity strip small multiples per marketplace: brand's own price versus marketplace price versus MAP line, per SKU with a flag

Not in the usual tools

  • claims tiered approved, site-backed or forbidden, tied to red lines and forbidden-claim detection, not generic content staleness
    vs Guru, Slite and MangoApps flag stale or unowned pages, but they tier general company docs, not ecommerce product and marketing claims against founder-set red lines
  • a conflict between a document and the live site resolved with a stated default (the site wins) rather than left for a person to notice
    vs enterprise wikis surface drift for a human to review; none apply a default-winner rule between two specific source types automatically
  • marketplace listings diffed against the brand's own price, stock and claims the same way the D2C site is crawled
    vs MAP-monitoring tools exist for large brands on Amazon, but nothing in this stack ties marketplace diffs to the same claims-tier and site-diff pipeline as the brand's own site
  • vendor and platform contracts extracted into renewal alerts sized to how long a renegotiation takes, inside the same brain as everything else about the brand
    vs generic contract-management SaaS tracks renewal dates but is not fed from or feeding the same brand brain a founder already uses for decisions
  • India-specific legal disclosure gate (grievance officer, country of origin, GST invoice fields, return policy) checked before a page ships, as a blocking check, not a general CMS compliance plugin
    vs generic CMS and Shopify legal-page apps exist, but they are not tied into this brand's own claims tiers and decisions log
  • a permission map enforced the same way whether a person opens the brain directly or asks a question in chat
    vs role-based access in most knowledge tools governs direct reads only; it is not applied to an AI chat answer drawing from the same sections

Used by founder, specialist owner, agency account manager. 12 jobs, 12 metrics, 11 insights computed in code, 7 actions.

What the room would say insights are rules, not opinions

View brand memory for this ideaNot nowREG-C03

Export decision history for handoffNot nowLOOP-C10

With no ideas approved yet, the room shows an empty kanban with a note that nothing is running, and the founder reason weight chart stays empty until at least 3 decisions repeat a pattern. The first thing to appear is the registry kanban itself, populated the moment the first card is approved; brand priors and the prediction check only build up after a few read points land.

Questions this room answers

  • what are we actually testing right now
    see everything currently running, and what each one is testing · REG-C03
  • what result am I waiting on, and is anything late
    see which reads are due and which are overdue · REG-C08, REG-C02
  • did it work, in numbers I can read in one line
    see the plain-word result the moment a read point lands · REG-C03
  • does this idea actually work for this brand, based on our own history
    see the brand's own updated belief about an idea after it was tried here · REG-C04
  • what did we put off, and is it time to look again
    see what was parked and why, and when that reason expires · REG-C05
  • are two experiments about to step on each other
    catch two running tests that would contaminate each other before either's read · LOOP-C35
  • did we actually agree what winning and losing look like before we started
    know every idea has an agreed metric, read point, and kill or scale rule before it starts · LOOP-C08
  • why is a web idea beating a bank idea, or vice versa
    see why the ranked list put one candidate above another, regardless of where it came from · LOOP-C09
  • why does the system already know I don't like ideas like this
    see every founder reason turned into a standing weight, without repeating myself · LOOP-C10
  • would the top pick actually be my pick
    see an internal check on whether the ranked pick matches what I'd actually approve · LOOP-C36
  • what did we learn, and would we do it again
    run the same retro shape every time an initiative closes · LOOP-C25
  • why did we do it this way, for someone new joining
    hand a new person the reasoning behind past decisions, not just the outcomes · LOOP-C10

How it is drawn

  • registry kanban kanban-style board: entries grouped by status: running, waiting, parked, done, with brand tags for an agency view
  • read point timeline horizontal timeline: upcoming and overdue read points across all running entries
  • brand prior sparkline small multiples of sparklines per idea: how the brand's own effect estimate for an idea has moved across repeated attempts
  • overlap matrix matrix / network diagram: which running entries share an audience or window with which others
  • founder reason weight chart horizontal bar chart: the most common founder reasons and the ranking weight each has earned
  • retro scorecard checklist-style card: what worked, what did not, and the would-we-again answer for each closed initiative

Not in the usual tools

  • a per-brand prior effect size for a marketing idea, built only from what happened when this brand tried it, separate from the bank's brand-agnostic basis
    vs GrowthBook, Eppo and VWO run and score individual A/B tests but do not carry a cross-idea, cross-attempt brand memory tied to a founder-facing idea bank
  • a founder's repeated approve, reject or park reason turned automatically into a ranking weight, without being asked to restate it
    vs decision logs in Notion or similar tools store the reason as text; nothing turns a repeated reason into a scoring input for the next similar idea
  • an overlap check across marketing ideas from a bank, an audit and the web, not just inside one engineering team's own test list
    vs GrowthBook's namespace collision checks cover its own hosted experiments, not ideas gathered from three different sources with different owners
  • the same retro shape (what worked, what did not, would we do it again) applied automatically to any closed initiative type: launch, crisis, onboarding, replatform
    vs most retro tools are generic templates a team fills in by hand per project; nothing here triggers automatically from a registry close event and files the answer as a reusable checklist

Questions this room answers

  • what happened this week and what needs my call
    read the whole week's state of the business in under 5 minutes · LOOP-C01, LOOP-C01
  • are we actually going to hit what I said mattered this quarter
    check whether this quarter's goals are on track, not just at quarter end · LOOP-C07
  • what do I show this client this month
    send a brand's monthly report when an agency runs it · ORCH-C07
  • across every client, where is the agency's time actually going, and is that right
    see every brand an agency runs on one portfolio view, not tab by tab · LOOP-C21
  • does this agency's report match what we actually measure
    check an agency or platform's own report against the brand's real numbers before trusting it · LOOP-C41
  • did we actually get what this invoice is charging for
    check a vendor or freelancer invoice against what was actually delivered · LOOP-C26
  • what do I tell my board or investors this quarter
    draft a board or investor update from the quarter's real numbers · LOOP-C11
  • are we having the right meetings, not too many or too few
    see whether the standing meeting cadence still fits the business as it grows · LOOP-C20
  • is anyone about to drop something because they have too much on
    see if any agency staff member or team member is overloaded across brands · LOOP-C21
  • where should the next rupee of budget go, across channels
    decide next month's channel budget split before any specialist spends their own slice · LOOP-C03

How it is drawn

  • weekly one-pager single-page summary card: headline number, what shipped, what is blocked, decisions needed this week
  • OKR progress bars horizontal progress bars: each key result against its target, pace-adjusted
  • monthly client report cards metric cards per brand: spend, ROAS, orders, revenue for the reporting month, per brand exists today
  • portfolio capacity heatmap heatmap grid: each agency person (rows) against each brand (columns), shaded by hours booked
  • invoice reconciliation table table with match status: each invoice line, its matching registry artefact if any, and a flag if unmatched
  • operating cadence calendar weekly calendar grid: each standing recurring review, when it happens, who attends, what it covers

Not in the usual tools

  • a weekly review that names the one number that moved most and why, tied to the registry's reads due and the decision log, not a static KPI list
    vs Shopify's own weekly dashboards and apps like Second Founder show revenue, orders, sessions and AOV, but do not tie the read to what is running in an experiment registry
  • a portfolio capacity heatmap showing hours booked per agency person across every brand they touch
    vs AgencyAnalytics and Whatagraph report campaign performance per client; neither shows a person's booked hours across the brands they serve
  • an agency or platform's own report reconciled line by line against the brand's own source-of-truth metric contract, with window mismatches and vanity metrics named
    vs reporting tools display what a platform or agency sends; none of them independently recompute and compare it to a separate source of truth
  • scope-of-work cadence tracked over multiple periods, not just whether one invoice matches one deliverable
    vs invoicing and project tools check a single invoice against a single delivery; a repeated under-delivery pattern across periods is not a built-in check
  • a standing meeting cadence that is itself reviewed for overload as the specialist set grows, rather than fixed once and left alone
    vs calendar and meeting tools schedule what is put in; none of them flag when the cadence itself has outgrown what a solo founder can sit through

Before any run, every metric shows unavailable and the room says 'no speed run on file yet, ask the agent to capture a baseline'. The moment the first baseline lands, the room shows the baseline load time, the LCP element, and the top 3 insights ranked by KB or ms saved, even before any fix has shipped. The fix chain and filmstrip only appear once a fix has actually been measured, not proposed.

    fix the largest paint element · SITE-C26

    make add-to-cart, filters and search feel instant · SITE-C27

How it is drawn

  • fix chain step chart: load time after each fix, cumulative, in shipping order exists today
  • before and after filmstrip frame strip: screenshots at fixed time steps, baseline versus the fixed page exists today
  • request waterfall by party waterfall chart: every request, colored first-party versus third-party, ordered by start time
  • page-weight budget trend line chart with a budget line: page weight and script count at every deploy or scheduled check against the stored budget
  • script inventory table sortable table: every script, its route, weight, blocking status and whether revenue ties back to it
  • cost of slowness bar bar chart: estimated monthly rupees lost at current LCP versus at target LCP, for the pages that carry the most traffic
  • sale-readiness checklist step chart or checklist with pass/fail dots: each dynamic feature, its last load-test result and days left to the next sale

Not in the usual tools

  • measures a real before-and-after per fix on a mirrored copy of the live page, with a filmstrip
    vs Google PageSpeed Insights gives a lab score and generic advice on today's page only, with no isolated fix chain
  • one Lighthouse-style score is replaced with a fix-by-fix chain a founder can stop trusting or not, item by item
    vs Shopify's Online Store Speed report is a single weighted score across the top 3 pages, not a per-fix breakdown
  • cross-references every heavy script against the brand's own revenue attribution before calling it dead weight
    vs also in Thunder and similar Shopify speed-and-app audits, which rank apps by measured weight but do not check revenue
  • turns a diagnosed fix straight into a brief with a pass threshold and a rollback plan, ready for a developer
    vs PageSpeed Insights and Lighthouse CI report the problem; they do not write the fix instructions or the rollback plan
  • prices slowness in the brand's own rupees per month, not a generic industry percentage
    vs no common speed tool ties LCP to the brand's own conversion rate and average order value
  • regression checks point back to the exact fix chain and filmstrip already on file, so a regression names its own cause
    vs also in Lighthouse CI and SpeedCurve for budget-based regression alerts, without the same fix-level history to compare against

Before any page has been audited, the room shows unavailable on every metric and a single line: 'no page has been scored yet, ask the agent to audit the top 3 ad landing pages first'. Once the first audit lands, the room leads with the message-match trace for the highest-spend ad, since a broken promise is the costliest and fastest thing to fix. The test backlog and results charts only populate once a test has been designed or launched; until then that panel says how many hypotheses are waiting to be scored.

How it is drawn

  • funnel by page step or sankey chart: sessions moving from landing page to PDP to cart to checkout to purchase, for the current page set
  • test backlog ranked list horizontal bar list: each hypothesis with its expected profit ceiling and evidence count
  • message-match trace flow diagram: the ad's promise elements carried across to the page and checkout, with breaks marked in red
  • hero score card scorecard with sub-scores: the hero's proof, headline, subhead, CTA and FUD scored against the fixed 6-part order
  • test result chart bar chart with confidence interval: conversion rate per arm with its confidence interval and sample ratio check
  • winner LTV check line chart, two cohorts: return rate and repeat-purchase rate for the winner cohort versus control, day 0 to day 60
  • PDP first-screen map annotated screenshot: the brand's real PDP screenshot on its own device with the 5 required elements marked present or missing

Not in the usual tools

  • audits whether the ad's exact promise, price, guarantee and CTA wording survives unbroken from the ad to the page to checkout
    vs VWO and Convert.com report test statistics well but do not check message continuity from the ad itself
  • ranks the test backlog by an expected-profit ceiling built from at least 2 pieces of evidence per hypothesis, before anything launches
    vs also in VWO's and Convert's own prioritization frameworks (PIE, ICE style scores), which score persuasion and ease but not a rupee ceiling tied to the brand's own traffic and AOV
  • checks a scaled winner's return rate and repeat-purchase rate before calling it a lasting win
    vs test platforms report the conversion lift at the moment of the test; none of the common tools re-check returns and repeat orders 60 days later
  • flags an urgency or scarcity claim as false the moment its displayed number stops matching real inventory or a real promo end date
    vs no common CRO or page-builder tool checks a countdown or stock count against the brand's own live feed
  • ties funnel drop-off to the page-routing decision (PDP versus advertorial versus quiz versus sales page) for that SKU's awareness stage
    vs also in GA4's funnel exploration for the raw drop-off numbers, which does not connect the drop to a routing rule or recommend a different page type

funnel_step_conversion
what share of visitors move from one step to the next		sessions reaching step N+1 divided by sessions reaching step N, for landing page to PDP to cart to checkout to purchaseanalytics events (GA4 or the brand's pixel) joined to page_registryrolling 30 days per page
analytics is not connected for that page		the brand's own trailing 90-day median per step, not an industry number
test_expected_profit_ceiling
the most this test could realistically be worth if it wins		estimated lift range x affected traffic x average order value x margin, capped at a stated ceiling assumptionSITE-C49 backlog scoringat the time the hypothesis is scored
the hypothesis has fewer than 2 pieces of supporting evidence, which the rule requires before scoring		none; used to rank the backlog, not compared to an external number
test_sample_size_required
how many visitors this test needs before its result can be trusted		computed from the chosen minimum detectable effect, baseline conversion rate and the test's statistical engineSITE-C50 test designset once at test design
no minimum detectable effect has been set		none; a design input, not a pass or fail number
days_to_significance_at_current_traffic
how many more days this test needs at today's traffic		(sample_size_required minus visitors so far) divided by average daily visitors to the testlive test traffic joined with test_sample_size_requiredupdated daily while a test is running
the test has not launched yet		none; a planning number
sample_ratio_mismatch_flag
whether traffic split evenly between the test arms the way it was set up to		chi-square check of visitor counts per arm against the configured splittest platform export or SITE-C52 readchecked at every read of results
fewer than 100 visitors per arm		pass at p over 0.01; anything tighter is flagged before any result is trusted
device_split_of_test_traffic
how the test's visitors split between mobile and desktop		count of test sessions grouped by device typetest platform exportwhile a test is running and at its final read
test has not launched		should roughly track the page's normal device split; a big gap is flagged
novelty_decay_pct
how much of the lift was just people noticing something new		lift in week 1 of the test minus lift in the weeks after, divided by week 1 lifttest platform export, weekly cohort readcomputed once a test has run 3 or more weeks
test has run fewer than 3 weeks or has under 100 conversions per arm		under 20 percent decay is treated as a real, durable win
winner_ltv_return_check
whether a scaled winner still looks good after returns and repeat orders are counted		compare 30 and 60 day return rate and repeat-purchase rate of the winner cohort against the control cohortSITE-C53, sales data joined to the test's order list30 and 60 days after a winner ships to 100 percent
fewer than 60 days have passed since rollout, or order-level attribution to the test is missing		no worse than the control's return and repeat rate
landing_page_load_status
whether the page this room is scoring is also fast enough to run traffic to		pulled from the Site and speed room's fully_loaded_mobile and page_weight_budget_status for the same pagespeed_lab.measure run table (shared with Site and speed)latest speed run for that page
no speed run recorded for that page		under 5 s on slow 4G
urgency_claim_truth_status
whether a displayed stock count or countdown is wired to a real number		compare the displayed value against the live inventory or promo end-date feed at the same timestamppage scrape joined to inventory feedchecked daily on live pages carrying an urgency element
the page has no urgency element, or no inventory feed is connected		match; any mismatch is flagged, not scored
proof_placement_coverage
how many of the page's objections have proof placed next to them		count of objections with a mapped proof element within the same page section, divided by total objections on the brand's objection listSITE-C40 proof mapcurrent live page
no objection list exists yet for that SKU		every objection covered
Data this room needs (8 tables)

  • ad_page_checkout_trace ad_id, page_id, promise_elements, match_status, checked_at · SITE-C36 audit script · weekly and on any ad or page change
  • funnel_events session_id, step, page_id, device, timestamp · GA4 or the brand's own pixel export · daily
  • cro_test_registry test_id, hypothesis, evidence_count, expected_profit_ceiling, mde, sample_size_required, stopping_rule, status, launched_at · SITE-C49 and SITE-C50 design output · on creation and status change

Store health

Used by founder, developer, agency account manager, operations lead. 14 jobs, 20 metrics, 15 insights computed in code, 15 actions.

What the room would say insights are rules, not opinions
{item} is marked done but was never actually tested the way a customer would use it
rule: a launch checklist item is marked done but its own verification step (the action a real customer would take) has not been logged as passed
Approve pre-launch QA and go-liveNot nowSITE-C14
{app} costs {inr} a month and nothing in your sales data ties back to it

Send to developerNot nowSITE-C59

Before anything is connected, the room shows unavailable across the board and one line: 'no monitoring set up yet; ask the agent to run pre-launch QA or set up uptime checks first'. The first things to populate are the launch checklist (if the store has not launched) or the uptime and checkout status (if it has), since a down store or a broken checkout outranks every other finding here. COD and RTO metrics only appear once order-level logistics status is fed in; until then that panel says what data is missing to turn it on.

Questions this room answers

  • is the account, staff access and 2FA set up the way it should be
    set up the store foundation and account security · SITE-C02
  • does every payment method actually complete a real order
    configure and go-live-test payment gateways · SITE-C09
  • which apps are we paying for and running that earn nothing back
    audit and govern the app and script stack · SITE-C13
  • is every launch step actually verified, not just checked off
    run pre-launch QA and launch sequencing · SITE-C14
  • what quietly broke since the last time someone looked
    monitor post-launch store hygiene · SITE-C15
  • is checkout fast, safe and free of settings that cost sales
    configure cart and checkout base settings · SITE-C18
  • which of these findings are not actually a site problem
    route out-of-scope findings to the right specialist · ORCH-C03
  • can a keyboard or screen-reader shopper actually buy something here
    audit and remediate accessibility · SITE-C59
  • can search and AI answer engines actually read our product pages
    implement structured data and schema markup · ORG-C23
  • did the last change break browsing, cart or a discount code
    run continuous regression testing on theme and app changes · SITE-C64
  • is the store up and can someone actually complete an order right now
    run real-time uptime and transaction monitoring · SITE-C65
  • which COD orders are likely to bounce back before we ship them
    reduce COD return-to-origin with checkout-side controls · SITE-C67
  • is what we are testing on staging actually what is live
    keep a staging environment in sync with production · SITE-C70
  • do our tracking scripts actually wait for consent, not just show a banner
    gate tracking scripts by consent for DPDP and GDPR · SITE-C78

How it is drawn

  • launch checklist tree checklist with pass/fail per item: every launch item, whether it is done and whether it is verified
  • uptime and incident timeline timeline with incident markers: uptime and checkout success over the last 30 days with incidents marked and their duration
  • app and script inventory table sortable table, color-coded: every app with cost, weight and revenue link, red flagged when it has none
  • accessibility issue heatmap table heatmap, templates by rule: which WCAG rule fails on which template
  • structured data coverage bar stacked bar by page type: share of eligible pages with valid, invalid or missing structured data, per page type
  • RTO funnel by pincode tier funnel or stacked bar: COD orders placed, confirmed, delivered and returned to origin, split by pincode tier
  • staging drift indicator single stat with a trend line: days since staging last matched production, over time

Not in the usual tools

  • checks a real transaction path end to end (add to cart through payment) from several regions, not just whether the homepage returns a 200
    vs UptimeRobot and StatusCake ping a URL and report status; they do not run a synthetic checkout
  • ties every accessibility violation to the template it repeats on and routes it straight to a developer brief
    vs also in axe DevTools and WAVE for the automated scan itself, which report a flat issue list without grouping by template or assigning it anywhere
  • tracks structured data coverage as one rolling percentage across every eligible page type, not one URL at a time
    vs also in Google's Rich Results Test and Search Console for validating a single page; neither gives a store-wide coverage number
  • reads the RTO outcome back against the brand's own delivered-order history to say which COD control actually reduced returns
    vs also in COD Guard, Level and RTO Shield for running the checkout-side block or confirmation; they report that the control ran, not whether it worked for this brand
  • cross-references every installed app against the brand's own revenue attribution before calling it dead weight
    vs also in Thunder's and similar Shopify app-and-speed audits for counting and weighing apps; they do not check revenue
  • verifies each pre-launch checklist item with the action a real customer would take, not a box someone ticks from memory
    vs a manual launch checklist (a spreadsheet or a platform's own launch guide) records that a step was done, not that it was proven

What the room would say insights are rules, not opinions
{ad_name} looks worn out. CTR is down {ctr_pct}% and its audience has stopped growing.
rule: an ad's CTR is down 25% or more and ROAS is down 30% or more over the last 7 days versus the prior 21, AND its ad set's reach in the last 7 days grew less than 10% versus the 7 days before: call it worn out. If reach still grew 10% or more while CTR or ROAS fell, call it a targeting or sequencing problem, not fatigue.
Revive with a new hook or format swapNot nowCREA-C15
{ad_name}'s hook stops {hook_pct}% of viewers, above your best quarter of ads, but it is not converting. Try the same hook with a different offer.
rule: an ad's hook_rate is in the top quartile of the account's trailing 90 days, but its verdict is Watch or Cut
Build or grade a hook bankNot nowCREA-C04
{ad_name} spent {spend} at {roas}x with no breakdown on file. Write one before the next batch is commissioned.
rule: an ad in the bottom spend quartile has spent at least one target CPA, its ROAS is under half the target, and it has been live 14 days or more with no saved breakdown
See full breakdownNot nowCREA-C16
{n} live ads have no brief on file, including {ad_name}.
rule: an ad is currently spending and has no brief_id recorded in the brief log
Write the briefNot nowCREA-C06
{shipped} of {planned} planned assets have shipped with {days} days left this month.
rule: assets shipped this month are under 70% of the planned count with 5 or fewer days left in the month
Add to the production calendarNot nowCREA-C18
Cost per finished asset is {cost}, {pct}% above your 3-month average.
rule: this month's cost per produced asset is more than 1.3 times the trailing 3-month average
Log this cost against the budgetNot nowCREA-C54

Before any brief, cost or rights log exists, the room shows only what the Meta gallery already tracks: ad-level winners, losers and the existing spend-based fatigue flag. The breakdown coverage, pipeline, cost and rights sections show 'not started, nothing logged yet' rather than 0 or blank. The first useful thing to appear, once a single brief is logged, is whether current spend is running against a written brief at all.

Questions this room answers

  • which ads should I copy the pattern of and which should I kill
    see which live ads are winning or losing and why, broken into hook, structure, proof, offer and CTA · CREA-C16, CREA-C42
  • is this ad fatigued or just underfunded
    tell a genuinely worn-out ad apart from one that just has a small, undertested audience · CREA-C15
  • what angle should the next batch of creative argue, and for whom
    turn customer language and personas into a testable angle before booking anything · BRAND-C14, BRAND-C11, CREA-C03
  • what exactly are we building, for whom, and why
    write the one-page brief before any shoot or design tool opens · CREA-C06
  • which hook actually stops the scroll
    build and grade a bank of opening hooks before scripting a full ad · CREA-C04
  • how many usable statics came out of this brief, and does the product shown match what ships
    produce static ad concepts and variants from an approved brief · CREA-C07, CREA-C12
  • does this script hit hook, mechanism, proof, objection and CTA, and stay an argument, not a biography
    script and storyboard video, VSL and founder-led ads in the proven order · CREA-C08, CREA-C09
  • which creators are worth briefing again, and are we cleared to run their post as a paid ad
    source, vet and authorize UGC and whitelisted creator content · CREA-C10, CREA-C38, CREA-C51

    generate a finished AI-actor video end to end without booking a shoot · CREA-C36
  • does this concept fit the platform and the market, or does it need a redo

    turn one approved master into every placement, caption and length automatically · CREA-C39, CREA-C40, CREA-C41

    flag catalogue and rights risk before anything goes live at scale · CREA-C52, CREA-C43, CREA-C49, CREA-C52
  • what are competitors testing that we are not, and can I get approved creative into Ads Manager as a draft without touching a live setting
    study competitor creative for gaps, and stage approved work as paused drafts · LOOP-C13, CREA-C46, CREA-C47, CREA-C48

How it is drawn

  • winners and losers grid sortable card grid with thumbnails: every ad ranked by spend with verdict, ROAS, hook rate and a link to its breakdown exists today
  • fatigue diagnosis chart line chart, 3 lines plus an annotation: daily spend, rolling 7-day ROAS and link CTR, with the reach-growth line and the worn-out-or-not verdict marked
  • hook bank leaderboard ranked list with thumbnails or waveform for audio hooks: every hook in the bank, its grade, and which ads it has been tested in
  • production pipeline funnel funnel chart: briefs written, concepts made, assets shipped, ads launched, ads that hit Scale, in one month
  • cost per asset over time line chart with a budget line: monthly cost per finished asset against the planned budget per asset
  • rights and authorization calendar timeline: every licensed asset, stock clip and whitelisting window with its expiry date against currently live ads
  • competitor format and angle heatmap matrix, own volume vs competitor volume: each format and angle combination, this account's live count against competitor count

Not in the usual tools

  • Root-causes fatigue as a worn-out audience versus an under-tested budget, by checking whether the ad set's own reach has actually stopped growing
    vs Meta Ads Manager's native fatigue flag reports rising frequency and falling CTR only; it does not check whether reach growth has actually flattened for that ad set
  • Ties a written why-it-worked or why-it-failed hypothesis to this account's own numbers and its brief, for every winner and loser
    vs Foreplay and Motion tag ad elements like hook, format and angle automatically, but neither writes a hypothesis for this account's own result or links it back to the brief that produced it
  • Tracks real production cost per finished asset against the month's budget line and plan
    vs Triple Whale's Creative Cockpit reports ROAS, thumbstop rate and hold rate per creative, but has no cost-per-asset or pipeline-budget view
  • Flags stock, music, font and creator-whitelisting rights expiring on currently live ads before a takedown risk lands
    vs no creative analytics tool in this category tracks license or authorization expiry against ads that are actually live right now
  • Closes the loop on the pre-spend predictive score by checking it against this account's own real 14-day verdict
    vs predictive creative scoring tools report a score at upload time but do not check it later against what the ad actually did in this account
  • Flags a broken price or badge overlay at the SKU level on the rendered creative itself, before a DPA campaign goes live at scale
    vs Meta's Catalog diagnostics flag missing or malformed feed fields, not whether the price or badge overlay actually renders correctly on the ad

What the room would say insights are rules, not opinions
{n} lines in this set are flagged: {list}. None can ship until cleared.
rule: a claim check on a copy set returns any line matching a banned or unverified claim pattern: an unproven superlative, a named competitor comparison, or a guarantee that does not match the brand's actual policy
Run the claim checkNot nowLOOP-C12
{ad_name} promises {term}, but the page it sends to no longer says it.
rule: a live ad's offer, price or headline term no longer appears in any of its landing destination's current copy blocks
Re-check message match against the pageNot nowSITE-C39
The last 30 days of copy reads at grade {grade}, above the grade-10 limit.
rule: the median reading grade level of shipped copy over the trailing 30 days is above grade 10
Auto-edit for voice and reading-level rulesNot nowCREA-C26
{objection} is the number {rank} reason buyers hesitate, and no live line answers it.
rule: an objection ranked in the top 5 by frequency in the trailing 90 days has zero live ad or page line tagged to it
Write the objection lineNot nowCREA-C24
{offer_name} has waited {days} days for a yes or no.
rule: an offer copy draft has waited more than 5 business days without a recorded founder decision
Send this offer for founder approvalNot nowLIFE-C36
{pct}% of live prices are not framed the way you approved.
rule: more than 20% of live price mentions do not match the account's approved framing rule
Frame this priceNot nowCREA-C31

Before any claim tier, page-copy capture or QA log exists, the room shows only the ad copy already in the Meta gallery: headline, primary text and CTA, with no checks run. Claim check, message match and objection coverage sections show 'not started, no claim tiers or page copy on file' rather than a false pass. The first useful thing to appear, once claim tiers are entered, is which of today's live ads already fail one of them.

Questions this room answers

  • will this line get the ad rejected or make a promise we cannot keep
    check every claim, testimonial and guarantee line before it ships · LOOP-C12
  • does this ad still say the same thing as the page it lands on
    catch when a live ad's promise no longer matches the page it sends to · SITE-C39, SITE-C36
  • which text set is actually ready to test
    generate and grade headline and primary-text sets for a format · CREA-C05
  • is this draft too long, too fancy, or above the reading level we agreed on
    keep every draft inside the account's fixed writing rules automatically · CREA-C26, CREA-C26
  • does this line describe an offer we can actually honor, and is the price framed the way we approved
    turn an approved offer into copy, and frame its price honestly · LIFE-C36, CREA-C31
  • are we answering the objection that is actually costing us sales, before the smaller ones
    write bottom-funnel lines for the objections buyers actually raise, worst one first · CREA-C24
  • is this send inside the rules and does it match the account's cadence plan
    write SMS and lifecycle email copy blocks inside carrier, platform and cadence rules · LIFE-C14, LIFE-C14
  • can I get a starting set of ad copy today without waiting on a full brief
    draft a first-pass ad copy set straight from a live product page · CREA-C37
  • what do we say to move a cash-on-delivery order toward prepaid without sounding pushy
    write India-specific COD-to-prepaid and RTO-reduction lines · CREA-C44
  • is this listing or A+ module making a claim our ads are not allowed to make
    prep marketplace listing and brand-store module copy on the same claim tiers as ads · PROD-C34, CREA-C45
  • is this ad's copy written for the page it will actually land on, and is the upload sheet ready to submit
    route each ad's copy to the right destination and package an approved batch for bulk upload · SITE-C35, CREA-C35, CREA-C20
  • does this name carry the benefit or story on its own
    name a new product or system so the name itself does selling work · CREA-C32

How it is drawn

  • claim check ledger table: every claim check run, its status, the reviewer and the resolution
  • message match panel per live ad side-by-side text panel with matched terms highlighted: the ad's headline, offer and price terms next to the landing page's current hero and offer block, with matches and gaps marked
  • objection coverage matrix matrix, objections by channel: the top objections ranked by frequency down the side, ad, page, email and SMS across the top, checked where a live line exists
  • reading level trend line line chart with a ceiling line: median reading grade level of shipped copy over time against the grade-10 limit
  • offer approval funnel funnel or stage chart: offer drafts moving from drafted to submitted to founder decision, with days spent in each stage
  • price framing split bar chart: share of live prices by framing type against the approved rule
  • bulk upload QA checklist pass or fail row list per batch: every row in the last upload batch with its validation result before submission

Not in the usual tools

  • Checks every claim against this brand's own claim tiers and current platform policy before it ships
    vs Grammarly and Hemingway check grammar and readability only; neither knows this brand's approved claim tiers or platform ad policy
  • Scores message match by checking the ad's actual offer, price and headline terms against this brand's own current page copy blocks
    vs Instapage's AdMap gives a generic match score between an ad and its landing page, but it is not tied to this brand's own claim tiers or copy-block structure, and the page needs to be built inside Instapage
  • Ranks real customer objections from this account's own support tickets and reviews, then checks whether a live line actually answers each one
    vs no ad-copy tool ranks objections from a brand's own support data and checks copy coverage against that ranking
  • Catches an SMS or email draft breaking the opt-out, length or the account's own cadence rule before it ships, not after
    vs Klaviyo reports send performance after the fact; it does not block a draft for missing an opt-out or breaking a cadence rule before send
  • Flags a marketplace listing claim that is not in the same approved claim tier used in this brand's ads
    vs Amazon's own listing quality tools check keyword and image compliance, not claim consistency with the brand's ad copy
  • Tracks reading grade level and rule catches over time as a trend against this account's own writing rules
    vs Hemingway scores one paste at a time and keeps no per-account rule set or history

Before any connector is live, the room shows Meta-only numbers (already flowing through the existing gallery) and marks blended MER, TikTok ROAS, stock risk and competitor overlap as unavailable with a one-line reason each. The first thing that lands once the brand brain and stock feed connect is the MER bridge and the stock-pace risk flag, since those two catch the costliest mistakes (scaling on a wrong number, scaling into a stockout).

Questions this room answers

  • are we pacing to hit the month's budget and margin target
    know if today's spend and pacing are safe or a problem · MEDIA-C46, MEDIA-C47, MEDIA-C42
  • what is actually holding this account back
    find the single biggest constraint in the Meta account before spending more · MEDIA-C01
  • does Meta's own ROAS agree with what the brand actually made
    know if platform ROAS can be trusted before a scale call · MONEY-C23, MEDIA-C06, MEDIA-C56
  • which ad sets do I touch today and by how much
    decide what to kill, keep or scale right now · MEDIA-C17, MEDIA-C15, CREA-C15
  • is the account built the way it should be for our spend
    check the account structure and naming match the brand's spend tier · MEDIA-C09, MEDIA-C10, MEDIA-C11
  • are we wasting reach on bad placements or a bid strategy that fits the wrong stage
    check audiences, placements and bid strategy are set right · MEDIA-C12, MEDIA-C13, MEDIA-C14
  • is retargeting eating cold budget, and is DPA read the right way
    protect existing-customer spend and catalogue ads from the cold budget · MEDIA-C19, MEDIA-C20
  • why did yesterday's numbers move so much, and is it us or the market
    catch a spend or ROAS anomaly and find the cause fast · MEDIA-C45, MEDIA-C68, SITE-C36, BRN-C11
  • is our tracking clean and are we accepting Meta's suggestions blindly
    check pixel, CAPI and platform-suggestion health · MONEY-C21, MEDIA-C07
  • is TikTok set up, scaling and Shop-ready the way the playbook says
    run a TikTok launch, scale and Shop motion the right way · MEDIA-C24, MEDIA-C25, MEDIA-C26, MEDIA-C27, CREA-C20, MEDIA-C29
  • are we ready for the sale date and for buyers outside India
    plan a peak sale ramp, wind-down and cross-border delivery · MEDIA-C21, MEDIA-C22
  • is anything about to get an ad rejected or a login lost
    keep account access, pre-launch QA and policy risk clean · MEDIA-C23, MEDIA-C59, MEDIA-C55, MEDIA-C55
  • what is waiting on me right now and why
    see which decisions need the founder, not the agent · ORCH-C03, LOOP-C03, LIFE-C22, MEDIA-C07, MEDIA-C62, MEDIA-C63, MEDIA-C63

How it is drawn

  • pacing gauge gauge plus line: month-to-date spend vs planned budget, with a projected end-of-month line exists today
  • kill/keep/scale table table with verdict chips: every live ad set, its ROAS, spend and verdict, ranked by spend at risk
  • frequency by segment small multiples small line charts, one per audience segment: 30-day frequency trend per segment against cold reach
  • anomaly strip calendar heat strip: every day in the range shaded by how far spend or ROAS moved from the 14-day median
  • MER bridge waterfall: Meta ROAS, TikTok ROAS and blended MER side by side with the gap labelled
  • change log timeline vertical timeline: every structure, bid or targeting change with who made it and the before/after read
  • TikTok organic-to-Spark timeline step timeline: post date, 5 to 7 day organic read, Spark date, authorization expiry
  • marginal ROAS bars bar chart: the next-dollar return estimate per channel, side by side

Not in the usual tools

  • puts ad-set kill/keep/scale verdicts next to same-day stock level and competitor discount data, not performance numbers alone
    vs Meta Ads Manager and rule tools like Revealbot judge only spend, ROAS and frequency inside the ad account; they do not see stock or competitor moves
  • computes blended MER from the brand's own contribution margin and reconciles it against Meta's and TikTok's own reported ROAS every time before a scale call
    vs Meta Ads Manager and TikTok Ads Manager report only their own platform ROAS; also in Triple Whale for a blended revenue number, but Triple Whale's number is not tied back to the brand's own margin ledger
  • logs every Advantage+ or automated suggestion applied, with a persisted before/after read attached to it
    vs Ads Manager shows a suggestion feed but keeps no lasting before/after test log once a suggestion is applied
  • fans one spend or ROAS anomaly out to the brand's own site speed, stock level and competitor snapshot from the same day, automatically
    vs Meta Ads Manager and TikTok Ads Manager flag the anomaly inside the ad account but never look outside it for the cause
  • tracks each TikTok Spark creator's 365-day authorization next to the organic post that earned it, with the expiry clock visible
    vs TikTok Ads Manager shows Spark ads live but not the organic-to-paid loop timing or authorization expiry in one place
  • routes every founder-gate call (scale ceiling, WhatsApp COD rollout, new-surface pilot, agentic-bidding guardrail) into one queue instead of leaving it split across ad account settings
    vs no platform separates its own automation's suggestions from the calls only the founder should make

Before Google Ads is connected, the room shows nothing but a clear 'not connected' state on every metric, since the direct Marketing API is not active yet (only Meta is read-only connected today). The first thing to show once it connects is conversion_tracking_coverage and the scaling gate checklist, since a tracking gap or a failed gate protects against the worst decision (scaling on fake data or before the account has earned it).

Questions this room answers

  • where does this account actually break: business, channel, campaign or metric
    diagnose the account top down before changing anything · MEDIA-C02
  • is real order value passing through cleanly to Google
    confirm conversion tracking is actually right · MEDIA-C04
  • is Merchant Center compliant and are the free add-ons enrolled
    check Merchant Center is set up and healthy · MEDIA-C30
  • do our titles lead with what people actually search
    check the feed's title and content quality · MEDIA-C31
  • does every campaign have one funnel job and a written reason
    check the campaign architecture has a defendable reason for every split · MEDIA-C32
  • is brand spend inflating the numbers we use to judge non-brand
    check brand is split from non-brand everywhere · MEDIA-C33
  • is our Shopping structure right for our catalogue and margin spread
    read and run Shopping the right way for our catalogue size · MEDIA-C34
  • which keywords are wasting spend and which are ready for more
    read and run Search the right way · MEDIA-C35
  • which PMax defaults are hiding control from us right now
    control Performance Max instead of letting it run blind · MEDIA-C36
  • are Search and Shopping dialled in enough to add YouTube
    decide if YouTube or Demand Gen is ready to add · MEDIA-C37
  • are our lists big enough to use and are customers excluded from cold
    check remarketing and exclusion lists are built and sized right · MEDIA-C38
  • should we still be on Maximize Conversion Value or move to Target ROAS
    know what bid strategy stage we are in and if we are ready to move · MEDIA-C39
  • is our daily budget too small or too big for our AOV and stage
    size and pace budgets for our stage · MEDIA-C40
  • have we actually earned the right to add budget or a new campaign type
    pass the scaling gates before adding budget · MEDIA-C41

How it is drawn

  • channel-type funnel grouped bar: spend and ROAS for Search, Shopping, PMax and YouTube side by side
  • scaling gate checklist 5-light checklist: each of the 5 scaling gates as pass or fail
  • feed quality scorecard scorecard tiles: title quality %, open disapprovals, add-ons enrolled
  • bid strategy ladder step progress bar: current stage on the Maximize Conversion Value to Target ROAS ladder, with the volume needed for the next step
  • search terms waste table sortable table: search terms ranked by wasted spend, with a one-click negative-keyword action
  • budget sizing gauge gauge: today's budget against the formula's recommended range
  • brand vs non-brand split stacked bar: spend and ROAS for brand vs non-brand over time

Not in the usual tools

  • shows the 5 scaling gates as one pass/fail checklist before a scale recommendation is allowed through
    vs the Google Ads recommendations page scores a generic optimization score, not the brand's own gate rules
  • sizes budgets to the brand's own AOV and non-brand break-even CPA, not a generic recommended-budget number
    vs Google Ads' own budget suggestions are volume-based, not tied to the brand's margin
  • flags every Performance Max default that hides control (URL expansion, auto assets, final URL expansion) in one view
    vs these settings live in separate tabs inside Google Ads with no single audit view; also in Optmyzr's PMax audit for the same checks, at a subscription cost
  • reconciles Google's own reported ROAS against the brand's blended MER before any scale call, using the brand's own margin data
    vs Google Ads reports its own ROAS only and has no blended, cross-channel or margin-based view
  • checks feed titles against the brand's own converting search terms, not a generic title-length or keyword-stuffing checklist
    vs Merchant Center diagnostics flag policy violations, not title-to-search-term match
  • reads the PMax search terms report next to a bid-strategy-stage gate, so a brand does not move to Target ROAS before it has volume
    vs Google Ads shows the search terms report and the bid strategy recommendation in separate places with no combined readiness check

Before any connector is live, the room shows the marketplace brief status board (Amazon, Flipkart, quick commerce) as the one part that works today, since those briefs are handoff documents, not live reads, and marks every performance metric for those channels as unavailable with 'no connector yet' rather than a blank or a zero. Once the affiliate network and billing pulls connect, the first things to show are open fraud flags and invoice variance, since those two protect real money before anything else in this room does.

Questions this room answers

How it is drawn

  • affiliate funnel funnel chart: applied, approved, active, flagged counts
  • commission reconciliation table table: owed vs paid vs variance, per payout cycle, per affiliate above a threshold
  • billing reconciliation waterfall waterfall: platform-reported spend, invoiced amount and ledger amount for the period, with the gaps labelled
  • marketplace brief status board kanban-style status board: Amazon, Flipkart, Blinkit, Zepto and Instamart briefs by status (drafted, sent, live)
  • creator mention feed card feed: discovered organic mentions with engagement and whitelist status
  • DSP readiness gauge gauge: days of sustained retargeting saturation against the threshold that triggers a DSP evaluation

Not in the usual tools

  • flags affiliate fraud using fast click-to-conversion, suspect ISP, big-ticket-early-order and brand-bidding rules together, tied back to the exact commission owed
    vs also in Refersion for the vetting checks themselves, but Refersion flags fraud inside its own tracker and does not tie the flag to the brand's separate commission ledger
  • reconciles what an affiliate network says is owed against verified sales and refunds before a payout is approved
    vs Refersion auto-verifies and auto-approves commissions from its own tracker; this room checks that number against the brand's order data first
  • turns Amazon, Flipkart and quick-commerce plans into a handoff brief with targeting, budget and a category ROAS benchmark, and says plainly that live performance stays unavailable until those accounts are connected
    vs no dashboard here claims to read Amazon, Flipkart or quick-commerce ad performance; the room is honest about the gap instead of guessing
  • matches each platform's invoice, including GST and reverse-charge credits, against both its own reported spend and the brand's budget ledger in one reconciliation
    vs Meta, Google and TikTok billing centers show an invoice against their own reported spend only, not the brand's ledger or its GST credits
  • surfaces creators already wearing or reviewing the product organically as whitelist candidates before any outreach cost is paid
    vs affiliate and influencer platforms track creators already inside the program, not organic mentions found outside it
  • keeps founder-gate calls (fraud holds, payout disputes, DSP spend, commission tier changes) in one queue instead of split across the affiliate network's UI, each platform's billing page, and email
    vs no single tool spans affiliate, marketplace ad and platform billing decisions in one founder queue

With nothing connected, every metric shows unavailable and the room shows only the job list and empty chart frames, each with 'connect your ESP and order data to see this.' The first thing that lands once esp_sends and orders are connected is the flow revenue reconciliation chart and the consent-gap insight, since those need only 2 tables and catch the most urgent problems (money being miscounted, and sends that should not have gone out). WhatsApp-specific metrics stay unavailable until a WhatsApp Business or BSP feed is connected, which is normally the last piece for an Indian brand.

Questions this room answers

  • is my pop-up earning its keep or just annoying visitors
    design and test the on-site sign-up pop-up · LIFE-C02
  • am I legally allowed to send this message to this person right now
    keep consent and quiet hours correct in every market · LIFE-C03
  • is the welcome flow converting inside the first 72 hours
    turn new subscribers into first buyers · LIFE-C04
  • how much of my lost cart and browse traffic am I recovering, and does the discount fire too early
    win back browsers and cart abandoners · LIFE-C05, LIFE-C06
  • is my post-purchase content actually reducing returns and pulling a second order
    turn order 1 into order 2 · LIFE-C07
  • is win-back timed off this brand's own repurchase cadence or a generic default
    win back lapsed buyers and stop mailing dead contacts · LIFE-C08
  • are charge reminders, pause-or-skip and failed-payment messages actually landing before the problem happens
    keep subscriptions and replenishment on schedule · LIFE-C09
  • do VIP customers actually notice and use the perks
    run loyalty, VIP and birthday triggers · LIFE-C10
  • is every back-in-stock alert tied to a real restock, and is the review request timed after delivery, not shipment
    run back-in-stock and post-delivery education sends · LIFE-C11
  • are my segments still accurate or built on stale rules
    keep the segmentation model current · LIFE-C12
  • am I over-mailing discounts and under-mailing everything else
    plan the weekly campaign calendar · LIFE-C13
  • is my list quality putting future sends at risk of the spam folder
    protect sender reputation and list health · LIFE-C16
  • should this message be WhatsApp instead of SMS, and is our India WhatsApp commerce program converting COD to prepaid
    run SMS, WhatsApp and push as their own channels · LIFE-C17, LIFE-C22
  • what changed this month, is it a traffic problem or a flow problem, and what should I test next
    run the test program and monthly health read-out · LIFE-C19, LIFE-C20

How it is drawn

  • flow revenue reconciliation grouped bar chart: platform-reported vs order-matched revenue, per flow
  • deliverability trend line chart: inbox placement and spam complaint rate over the last 90 days exists today
  • flow step funnel step funnel chart: contacts at each step of a chosen flow: entered, delivered, opened, clicked, ordered exists today
  • list health meter gauge/meter: the composite list_health_score with its 3 inputs
  • test queue board kanban-style list with a small bar for win rate: tests planned, running, completed, and win/loss exists today
  • channel mix stacked bar chart: revenue and cost by email, SMS, WhatsApp and push, monthly
  • send calendar heatmap calendar heatmap: campaign and flow sends per day, colored by discount vs non-discount content

Not in the usual tools

  • Revenue by flow is counted from real orders matched to send logs, not from a click-attribution window that counts on send date and gives every touch its own credit
    vs Klaviyo's flow revenue reports revenue on the date the first message was sent, inside a configurable click window (default 5 days email, 1 day SMS), which can overstate or misdate a flow's real contribution
  • One consent and quiet-hours record checked before every send across email, SMS and WhatsApp together
    vs also in Klaviyo and Gorgias separately for their own channel, but neither merges consent across every channel one brand sends on before a send goes out
  • A pre-send honesty check that blocks a scarcity or countdown claim with no matching inventory or expiry record
    vs no ESP or SMS platform enforces this automatically; it is normally a manual copy-review step, if it happens at all
  • Flow revenue mismatch between the platform's own report and real orders is surfaced as its own alert
    vs ESPs report their own attributed number with no way to see the gap against actual order data without exporting both and joining them by hand
  • Test hygiene (minimum sample, pre-registered read window) is enforced across flows, campaigns and channels in one backlog
    vs Klaviyo's built-in A/B testing checks a single flow or campaign split, not a cross-channel test calendar with hygiene rules
  • WhatsApp COD-to-prepaid conversion is tracked as its own metric tied to order data
    vs not a standard metric in Klaviyo, Gorgias or generic WhatsApp Business dashboards, which report delivery and read rates, not payment-method conversion

MetricFormulaSourceWindow, unavailable whenBenchmark
revenue_by_flow_order_matched
Revenue each flow actually produced, counted from real orders, not from a click window		sum of order revenue where the order's contact received a flow send in the N days before the order (N set per flow type, default 5 days email / 3 days SMS-WhatsApp), attributed to the last flow send before the orderorders table joined to esp_sends logtrailing 30 and 90 days, by flow
orders and esp_sends cannot be joined on the same contact id		welcome flow typically 3 to 5 percent of total email/SMS revenue for a healthy list; no universal number, compare to this brand's own trend
flow_conversion_rate
Share of people who got a flow and then bought		orders attributed to flow ÷ unique contacts who entered the flow, trailing windowesp_sends + orderstrailing 90 days per flow
flow entry log missing for that flow		not applicable, compare within brand across flow versions
deliverability_inbox_placement
Share of sends that land in the inbox, not spam or missing		1 minus (bounce rate + spam complaint rate), proxy when no seed-test data existsESP bounce and complaint logs, seed-test tool if connectedtrailing 30 days
no bounce or complaint feed from the ESP		under 0.1 percent complaint rate, under 2 percent bounce rate is healthy
list_health_score
One number for whether the list is getting healthier or riskier to mail		composite of bounce rate, spam complaint rate, and share of contacts with 0 opens in 180 days, each normalized 0 to 1 and averagedesp_sends logtrailing 90 days, refreshed weekly
fewer than 4 weeks of send history		no fixed number, watch direction of travel
popup_conversion_rate
Share of visitors shown the pop-up who sign up		pop-up sign-ups ÷ pop-up impressionson-site capture tool event logtrailing 30 days, by variant
pop-up tool does not log impressions		2 to 4 percent visitor-to-signup is a common range; treat as a comparison, not a target
time_to_first_purchase
How long a new subscriber takes to buy for the first time		median hours between signup timestamp and first order for subscribers who convert within 30 daysconsent_log + orderstrailing 90 days of signups
signup timestamp not captured		shorter is better; compare against the brand's own last quarter
sms_whatsapp_opt_in_rate
Share of email subscribers who also opt in to SMS or WhatsApp		contacts with a valid SMS or WhatsApp consent record ÷ contacts with a valid email consent recordconsent_logcurrent snapshot
consent_log missing a channel		no universal figure, track growth over time
consent_coverage
Share of active contacts with a valid, current consent record for every channel they are sent on		contacts with a logged, unexpired consent record for channel X ÷ contacts sent on channel X in the windowconsent_log vs esp_sendstrailing 30 days
no consent_log		target 100 percent; any gap is a compliance risk, not a growth metric
list_growth_net
New contacts minus people who leave the list		new opted-in contacts − (unsubscribes + hard bounces + suppressions)consent_logmonthly
consent_log has gaps in the period		should stay positive; a negative month is a flag
unsubscribe_rate_per_send
Share of people who unsubscribe from a single send		unsubscribes ÷ delivered, per campaign or flow sendesp_sendsper send, rolled up weekly
send-level unsubscribe not logged		under 0.2 percent per send is typical; above 0.5 percent needs a content check
whatsapp_cod_to_prepaid_conversion
Share of WhatsApp cart-recovery or order-update messages that convert a COD order to prepaid		orders switched from COD to prepaid after a WhatsApp nudge ÷ COD orders that received the nudgewhatsapp_events + orderstrailing 30 days
no WhatsApp events log or no COD flag on the order		no fixed target; India benchmark not public, track the brand's own baseline before and after the program
quiet_hours_violation_count
Number of sends that went out outside the allowed local hours for that market		count of esp_sends where sent_at local time falls outside the configured quiet-hours window for the contact's marketesp_sends + consent_log market fieldtrailing 30 days
contact market or local time zone unknown		target zero
test_win_rate
Share of tests in the queue that produced a real, pre-registered win		tests marked won ÷ tests completed, where a win required hitting the pre-registered metric and sample sizetest_registrytrailing 6 months
test_registry missing a result field		no fixed number, watch trend and hygiene violations
segment_freshness
Days since a segment's definition was last recomputed against current data		today minus last_computed_at, per segmentsegments tablecurrent snapshot
segment has no last_computed_at		under 7 days for behavior segments
welcome_flow_completion_rate
Share of new subscribers who make it through the whole welcome sequence without unsubscribing		contacts reaching final welcome step ÷ contacts entering step 1esp_sends flow step logtrailing 90 days
step-level flow log not available		compare step to step, not to an outside number
winback_reactivation_rate
Share of lapsed contacts in the win-back flow who place another order		orders from win-back flow contacts ÷ contacts entering win-backesp_sends + orderstrailing 180 days
flow entry not logged		no universal figure, track vs the brand's own prior version
flow_frequency_conflicts
Number of contacts who got messages from 2 or more flows on the same day		count of contact-days where esp_sends shows sends from more than one flow_idesp_sendstrailing 30 days
flow_id missing from send log		target near zero, some overlap is unavoidable at high volume
channel_revenue_mix
Share of lifecycle revenue coming from email vs SMS vs WhatsApp		order-matched revenue per channel ÷ total order-matched lifecycle revenueesp_sends + orderstrailing 90 days
channel field missing on a send		no fixed split, watch for over-reliance on one channel

What the room would say insights are rules, not opinions
{n} customers are past their usual reorder window with zero contact from us or to us
rule: a customer's RFM recency decays past their own historical repurchase window, with no support contact and no email/SMS engagement in the same period
Let the agent rescore churn risk and route savesNot nowLIFE-C33

With nothing connected, the room shows the job list and unavailable states everywhere except cx_maturity_score, which only needs a manual scoring session against a written standard. The first real signal once orders and refunds are connected is repeat_purchase_rate and return_rate; true_customer_profitability and cohort_gross_profit_ltv need support_tickets and product_costs on top of that, so they light up later. DPDP and GST reconciliation fields are the hardest to get since they live outside any marketing or CX tool, in accounting and a manual compliance log.

How it is drawn

  • cohort profit curve cohort heatmap / line chart: cumulative gross-profit LTV by acquisition month, at 30/60/90/180/365 days
  • WISMO and delivery funnel funnel chart: orders shipped, orders delayed, WISMO tickets raised, tickets resolved
  • RTO by zone map bar chart or choropleth by pincode cluster: COD RTO rate by geography and courier
  • support reason breakdown stacked bar chart over time: ticket volume by reason tag, monthly exists today
  • churn risk tiers funnel or stacked bar: customers per risk tier and where each tier gets routed (win-back, cancel-save, no action)
  • true profitability by tier waterfall chart: revenue minus returns, support, shipping and discount cost, by loyalty tier
  • NPS/CSAT trend line chart with driver tags: NPS and CSAT over time, annotated with the top driver reason each period exists today

Not in the usual tools

  • Lifetime value is computed on gross profit after returns, support and discount cost, not on revenue
    vs Shopify's native LTV and cohort reports are revenue based and, outside Shopify Plus, do not offer cohort analysis at all; Triple Whale's cohort tools are also built on revenue and ad spend, not on returns and support cost netted out
  • COD RTO and failed-delivery (NDR) reschedule outcomes are tracked as their own funnel tied to real courier events
    vs not a standard view in Klaviyo, Gorgias or Shopify analytics, which do not carry courier delivery-attempt data
  • Support ticket reasons and CSAT are shown next to return, RTO and true-profitability data in one room
    vs also in Gorgias for ticket reasons and CSAT alone; Gorgias has no view of returns cost, RTO or per-customer profitability
  • A single DPDP data-request register that tracks every system holding a person's data, with a legal SLA clock
    vs no ecommerce tool ships this off the shelf, since the data spans the ESP, helpdesk, loyalty app, POS and marketplace accounts a brand uses
  • One merged customer identity across guest checkouts, phone, WhatsApp numbers, POS and marketplace orders before churn or LTV is scored
    vs Shopify and most ESPs treat a guest checkout, a repeat email and a marketplace order as separate records unless a paid CDP is added; this identity resolution is a prerequisite step here, not an add-on
  • An AI support agent's exact autonomy limits (refund cap, allowed action types) are logged and checked against every action it actually takes
    vs off-the-shelf AI helpdesk agents surface a config screen for the limits, but few log and flag when an action approaches or crosses that limit automatically

true_customer_profitability

support cost or shipping cost cannot be allocated per order		compare tiers to each other, not to an outside number

With nothing connected, the room shows the job list, empty chart frames and one usable tile: the margin waterfall template, which a founder can fill by hand before any data is connected since it only needs a proposed price, COGS and discount. The first real numbers to land once orders and product_costs are connected are margin_after_discount and discount_dependency_rate. Competitor and MAP pricing depend on the agent's own public web pulls and are the hardest to keep current, since prices change without notice and matching a competitor's SKU to the brand's own is manual work the first time.

How it is drawn

  • margin waterfall waterfall chart: revenue down to net margin for a chosen offer or discount, step by step exists today
  • discount dependency trend line chart: share of revenue at a discount, monthly, over the trailing 12 months
  • free shipping threshold sensitivity small interactive chart or table: break-even threshold recalculated as AOV, margin or shipping cost inputs change
  • competitor price index line chart: brand price index versus each named competitor over time
  • bundle ladder funnel funnel or bar chart: share of orders choosing each tier in the ladder, single item to largest bundle
  • offer test backlog board kanban-style list with win rate: offer tests planned, running, done, with hygiene flags exists today
  • gift card liability vs redemption dual bar/line chart: outstanding liability and redemption rate over time

Not in the usual tools

  • The free shipping threshold is computed straight from this brand's own live AOV, margin and shipping cost, and flagged stale automatically when those inputs move
    vs standalone calculators such as Funnelytics or Cartylabs use the same break-even formula but need the numbers typed in by hand each time, with no link to the brand's own order data and no automatic re-check
  • A margin waterfall runs before an offer launches, and a founder-set red line blocks approval if it is crossed
    vs no researched tool ties a margin model to a hard approval gate before an offer goes live; discount apps in Shopify show usage after the fact, not a pre-launch margin check
  • Discount dependency and price integrity are tracked as one running score across every offer and channel, not just a discount-usage count
    vs Shopify's and most ESPs' discount reports show redemption counts and revenue per code, not a trained-to-wait signal across the whole account
  • Competitor and MAP price tracking is tied directly into the brand's own offer test backlog and margin data
    vs standalone MAP-monitoring services flag violations but do not connect to the brand's own cost and margin data to show what a match or hold would actually cost
  • Offer tests are checked for overlap (same segment, same window, two variables at once) automatically before a founder is asked to read results
    vs most brands track offer tests in a spreadsheet with no automatic overlap check, so a confounded result can look clean
  • Gift card liability and redemption are shown together as a cash-flow signal, not just a balance
    vs Shopify's native gift card report shows a balance, not a trend of liability growth against redemption pace
