With nothing connected yet, every metric shows unavailable and the room shows only the calendar plan and a checklist of what to connect (platform insights, UTM/promo tracking, the claims gate log, the seeding tracker, the community roster). The first thing that lands once platform insights connect is posting_floor_adherence and the calendar heatmap; revenue_from_tagged_content only appears once posts carry a UTM tag or promo code, which the room should prompt the team to add before the first campaign.

Questions this room answers

  • did we post what we planned and is the next festival slot covered
    know if this week's content plan held the posting floor and covers the next festival · ORG-C02
  • how much of what worked needed the founder's face, and are we over that budget
    decide how much the founder needs to be on camera this month · ORG-C03
  • what do we say, to whom, and in which language variant
    turn today's idea into a shootable script with a named SKU and buyer · ORG-C04
  • which post should become a format the team can run without the founder
    spot a one-off winner worth turning into a named repeatable series · ORG-C05
  • what do we need to film this batch and when do we raise production stakes
    plan the next shoot day without new gear or a new location · ORG-C06
  • what should we post about next, and what are named competitors doing
    keep the idea pipeline full without starting from zero every week · ORG-C07
  • is this draft ready for a human rewrite, and does it clear the claims and safety gate
    get an AI-assisted first draft that a human must rewrite and clear before it can publish · CREA-C21, LOOP-C12
  • which messages need a human right now, and does this post's funnel actually capture a lead
    answer comments and DMs fast and turn a stranger into an owned contact · ORG-C09, ORG-C10
  • what UGC is sitting unused, and what is about to retire without being reused anywhere
    turn a verified review or unboxing into a post, and keep the library tagged and reused before it expires · ORG-C11, ORG-C13
  • does every live post's link work, match, and carry the right product tag
    stop a post from linking to a dead, mismatched or untagged commerce page · ORG-C12
  • who should get product for content, and did we get usage rights back
    decide which creators to seed this month and confirm gifting, rights and fraud checks ran · ORG-C14
  • which content made money, and which viral post reached strangers who never bought
    see which SKU and hook actually sold, not just which post got views · ORG-C16
  • are our videos titled, thumbnailed and chaptered to be found in YouTube search
    run the YouTube channel as its own surface with its own search mechanics · ORG-C38
  • is the community active, and do active members actually buy
    know if the owned community (WhatsApp or Discord) is worth the moderation time · LIFE-C53

How it is drawn

  • calendar heatmap calendar grid: planned vs published posts per day, with festival flags
  • SKU by hook revenue grid heatmap table: matched revenue for each SKU by hook type
  • comment to order funnel stepped funnel chart: comments to DMs to owned contacts to orders
  • creator seeding scorecard table with bars: creators seeded, cost sent, content delivered, yield
  • asset lifecycle timeline gantt-style bar per asset: shelf-life window and reuse events across PDP, email, DM and post-purchase
  • community health line line chart with overlay: weekly active member rate with matched-order rate overlaid

Not in the usual tools

  • ties each post to the order it produced, by SKU and hook, using the post's own link tag or promo code
    vs Metricool, Later, Hootsuite and Sprout Social show reach, engagement and (Hootsuite/Sprout) social listening, but none join a post to a matched order
  • runs a fixed claims and safety gate against the brand's own claim tiers before a post can publish
    vs scheduler approval flows (Hootsuite, Sprout Social) check for human sign-off, not a rule-based claims-risk check
  • tracks creator seeding cost, fulfillment and content yield against the same tagged asset library the calendar draws from
    vs creator/UGC platforms track shipments and creators, but do not connect yield to the brand's own content library or calendar
  • flags a stranger-blind viral post and a broad-reach, low-order hit by a fixed rule tied to the store's own orders
    vs platform analytics report reach and engagement; none of the mainstream schedulers flag a post as viral-but-not-selling against the brand's own order floor
  • tracks an asset's shelf life and reuse across PDP, email, DM and post-purchase flow before retiring it
    vs social schedulers manage the posting calendar only; they do not track reuse of the same asset across owned commerce surfaces
  • reads the owned WhatsApp or Discord community's active-member rate against matched store orders
    vs Later has no social listening at all; Hootsuite and Sprout Social track engagement inside the platform, not matched to the brand's own order data

Questions this room answers

    know which product pages are missing the SEO basics · ORG-C21
  • which collection pages have no FAQ, no reviews and no grid logic

    confirm the structured data on money pages actually validates · ORG-C23

    screen an SEO, link or AEO vendor pitch before signing · ORG-C33

How it is drawn

  • keyword rank movement line chart: count of page-1 keywords over time exists today
  • crawl health treemap treemap: pages sized by traffic, colored by health status (healthy, warning, broken)
  • content refresh priority table sortable table: pages ranked by revenue at risk, with the reason flagged
  • internal link graph snippet node graph: money pages and their inlink count, orphans highlighted
  • organic revenue vs total revenue stacked area chart: organic revenue against total store revenue over time
  • backlink velocity by tactic stacked bar chart: new referring domains per month, split by outreach tactic

Not in the usual tools

  • computes organic revenue from the store's own order ledger, not from GA4's own attribution model
    vs Search Console reports clicks, impressions, CTR and position only; GA4's channel reports do not reconcile to the store's own revenue number
  • sequences the refresh backlog by revenue at risk from the store's own order data, ahead of writing anything new
    vs Semrush and Ahrefs content-audit tools flag declining pages by traffic or backlinks, not by the brand's own order value per page
  • checks AI-crawler-specific access rules (GPTBot, PerplexityBot, Google-Extended) in the same audit as classic crawl health
    vs Screaming Frog crawls like a browser but does not evaluate AI-crawler access, and has a known blind spot versus how Googlebot itself renders JavaScript
  • flags a discontinued or out-of-stock page that 404s instead of redirecting, tied to the store's own product feed
    vs generic site crawlers flag 404s as a status code; they have no way to know which ones are the brand's own discontinued SKUs
  • screens a vendor's own pitch numbers against the brand's own analytics before the founder signs anything
    vs no SEO platform performs vendor due diligence; every tool here assumes the brand already hired the vendor

Questions this room answers

  • what does ChatGPT, Gemini, Perplexity and Google AI say about us today

    keep the same facts identical everywhere an engine can read them · ORG-C35

    know if a correction actually landed after fixing it · ORG-C36
  • would an AI shopping agent get our price, stock and delivery estimate right

    get a short weekly read on what changed for us and named competitors · BRN-C11

How it is drawn

  • share of voice by engine stacked bar chart: brand vs named competitor mentions, one bar per engine exists today
  • fact consistency matrix grid, facts by surface: each core fact against each tracked surface, matched or mismatched
  • correction lifecycle timeline step timeline: confirmed wrong fact, site fix, publisher fix, re-check pass or fail
  • agentic listing spot-check table table: field, expected value, what the agent returned, match or mismatch
  • weekly change ticker diff list: what changed this week for the brand and named competitors
  • sentiment trend by engine line chart: monthly sentiment score per engine exists today

Not in the usual tools

  • ties the facts page to a fixed re-check cadence that confirms a correction actually landed in the engine's answer
    vs Semrush's AI Visibility Toolkit and Ahrefs Brand Radar report current mentions, share of voice and sentiment; neither tracks whether a specific correction you shipped later landed in the engine's answer
  • checks what a shopping agent would tell a buyer about price, stock and delivery against the brand's own live feed
    vs Profound, Otterly and the Semrush/Ahrefs AI-visibility products track how engines describe the brand in conversational answers; none of them check an agentic shopping flow against the brand's own feed
  • scores fact consistency across site, schema, feed and directories side by side, before an engine ever asks
    vs AI-visibility tools check what an engine says; none of them cross-check the brand's own published facts against each other first
  • sequences page rewrites by the gaps a real, frozen prompt audit found for this brand
    vs Semrush's AI Search Optimizer gives prioritized content recommendations from crawled content and general AI-visibility data, not from a frozen prompt audit tied to this brand's own confirmed gaps
  • bundles a named competitor's changing AI narrative with their social and PR activity in one short weekly brief
    vs Ahrefs Brand Radar and Semrush track competitor share of voice as a running number; they do not produce a founder-readable weekly brief of what changed, combining PR, social and AI narrative together

How it is drawn

  • candidate funnel funnel chart: count of candidates at each stage: screened, researched, gated, shortlisted, go
  • trend vs saturation map scatter plot: trend_direction_score on one axis, competitive_saturation_count on the other, one point per category
  • SKU margin waterfall waterfall chart: price stepping down through COGS, shipping, duty, ad spend and returns to net contribution margin
  • review complaint bars horizontal bar chart: top recurring complaints from mined reviews, ranked by share of reviews mentioning them
  • ad spend timeline step chart: count of advertisers with a live ad on this product idea over the trailing 6 months
  • shortlist scorecard grid ranked table with score bars: each shortlisted candidate's score on every scorecard criterion, ranked exists today

Not in the usual tools

  • combines video-commerce demand, ad library spend and DTC review mining in one screen before any paid research tool is used
    vs Helium 10 and Jungle Scout read Amazon Brand Analytics and keyword demand only; they do not touch TikTok-style shop data, ad libraries or a brand's own margin gate
  • computes true contribution margin per SKU including allocated ad spend and returns, and checks it against the original gate model
    vs Shopify's native profit-by-SKU report is CM1 only: no ad spend allocation, no return cost, no way to compare against a pre-launch model
  • enforces a visible, fixed research time box per candidate so research cannot sprawl
    vs no product research tool tracks hours spent against a budget; that discipline is normally left to the researcher
  • ties a mined review price signal directly into the margin gate's pricing input
    vs review-mining browser extensions summarise sentiment as text; they do not feed a pricing or margin calculation
  • reads trend direction and competitor count together as one go/no-go signal
    vs Google Trends and similar tools show a growth curve alone, with no seller-count overlay

With nothing connected, every SKU shows stock cover, reorder point and landed cost as unavailable, and the room prompts to connect an inventory feed or WMS first, since reorder math needs real velocity. Once an inventory feed lands, the first live view is the stock cover heat grid; courier and supplier scorecards fill in only after a courier or supplier data feed is connected, and stay unavailable, not zero, until then.

Questions this room answers

  • which SKUs run out first
    see stock cover across DTC and marketplace stock per SKU · PROD-C22
  • what do I order, and how much, right now
    see the reorder point and recommended PO quantity per SKU · PROD-C22
  • what does this SKU actually cost me to land today
    see landed cost per SKU refreshed for the latest FX, duty and freight · PROD-C23
  • how exposed am I to one supplier or one country failing
    see supplier and country concentration risk · PROD-C23
  • which suppliers are slipping on time, defects or price
    see a running scorecard on every active supplier · PROD-C69
  • is my warehouse hitting the pick-pack-dispatch times it promised
    see fulfilment SLA performance from the 3PL · PROD-C53, PROD-C52
  • which courier should carry which zone right now
    see a courier scorecard by zone · PROD-C59
  • which non-delivered orders need action today
    work today's NDR queue before the attempt window closes · PROD-C56
  • did the courier pay me what it owes me
    see COD remittance reconciled against orders · MONEY-C52
  • what stock is turning into dead weight, and what do I do about it

How it is drawn

  • stock cover heat grid heat map: each SKU's stock_cover_days against its lead time, colour-coded from safe to breach
  • landed cost waterfall waterfall chart: base cost stepping up through freight, duty and FX to the final landed cost per unit
  • courier scorecard bars grouped bar chart: RTO rate, first-attempt delivery rate and TAT per courier, grouped by zone
  • supplier scorecard trend small multiples line chart: each active supplier's on-time, defect and price-stability score over the last 6 scoring periods
  • NDR funnel funnel chart: orders raised as NDR, attempted again, resolved, or converted to RTO
  • dead stock aging bars stacked bar chart: inventory value by SKU, stacked by age band, with the dead-stock threshold marked

Not in the usual tools

  • recomputes landed cost the moment FX, freight or duty changes, and flags when a margin gate needs a recheck because of it
    vs Cin7 and Katana calculate reorder points and safety stock from velocity and lead time; neither ties a landed cost change back to a margin recheck automatically
  • joins the courier scorecard to this brand's own COD reversal cost to decide NDR reschedule versus RTO acceptance per order
    vs Shiprocket and NimbusPost report courier-level RTO, NDR and COD data in aggregate; they do not weigh a reschedule against this brand's own unit economics per order
  • sizes safety stock against India-specific spike risk, including festive and gifting demand, in the same screen as the reorder point
    vs generic inventory tools size safety stock from velocity and lead time alone, without a festive calendar or COD-market RTO risk built in
  • ties a supplier's scorecard directly to its concentration share and backup-factory status in one view
    vs supplier scorecards in most ERPs track performance only; they are not shown next to how exposed the brand is if that supplier fails
  • combines safety stock, warehouse staffing and WMS load readiness against the festive calendar into one readiness percentage
    vs peak-season planning is normally a spreadsheet exercise split across ops, warehouse and sourcing with no single readiness number
All 19 metrics with formulas
MetricFormulaSourceWindow, unavailable whenBenchmark
stock_cover_days
How many days of stock are left at current sales pace		current stock units ÷ trailing 14-day average daily units soldinventory table joined to SKU saleslive, daily refresh
no inventory feed connected for that channel		compared against the SKU's own lead_time_days plus safety buffer, not an external number
reorder_point_units
The stock level that should trigger a new order		(average daily units sold × lead_time_days) + safety_stock_unitsreorder calculation tablerecalculated weekly or on a velocity or lead-time change
fewer than 30 days of sales history for the SKU		no external benchmark; brand-specific by SKU
safety_stock_units
Buffer stock held against demand spikes and delays		service-level factor × demand standard deviation × sqrt(lead_time_days)reorder calculation tablerecalculated weekly
fewer than 30 days of sales history		no external benchmark; service level is a brand setting
landed_cost_per_unit
What one unit actually costs after freight, duty and FX		base supplier cost + freight allocated per unit + duty + FX conversion at the rate on the invoice datelanded cost tablerecomputed on any FX, freight or duty change, else weekly
no recent invoice or FX rate on file for the SKU		compared against the price used in the SKU's margin gate, not an external number
supplier_concentration_share
Share of purchase volume from the single largest supplier		largest supplier's purchased units ÷ total purchased units, trailing 12 monthssupplier master table joined to PO historytrailing 12 months, refreshed monthly
fewer than 2 POs on file		no external benchmark; flagged internally above 60%
country_concentration_share
Share of purchase volume from the single largest sourcing country		largest country's purchased units ÷ total purchased units, trailing 12 monthssupplier master table joined to PO historytrailing 12 months
fewer than 2 POs on file		no external benchmark; flagged internally above 70%
supplier_scorecard_score
A supplier's combined on-time, defect and price-stability score		weighted score from on_time_delivery_pct, defect_rate_pct and price_change_frequency over the scoring periodsupplier scorecard tabletrailing 90 days, refreshed monthly
fewer than 2 completed POs with this supplier		ranked against the brand's other suppliers, not an external index
fulfilment_sla_adherence_pct
Share of orders the 3PL dispatched inside its promised time		orders dispatched within contracted SLA window ÷ total orders processed3PL or WMS dispatch logtrailing 7 and 30 days
no WMS or 3PL feed connected		compared against the SLA written in the 3PL contract
courier_rto_rate
Share of shipments a courier returns undelivered, by zone		RTO shipments ÷ total shipments for that courier and zonecourier scorecard table (shipment and delivery status feed)trailing 30 days
fewer than 30 shipments for that courier-zone pair in the window		compared across the brand's own couriers in the same zone, not an external figure
courier_first_attempt_delivery_rate
Share of shipments delivered on the courier's first attempt		shipments delivered on attempt 1 ÷ total delivered shipments, by courier and zonecourier scorecard tabletrailing 30 days
fewer than 30 shipments for that pair		compared across the brand's own couriers, same zone
courier_delivery_tat_days
Average days from dispatch to delivery, by courier and zone		mean of (delivered_at − dispatched_at) in days, by courier and zonecourier scorecard tabletrailing 30 days
fewer than 30 shipments for that pair		compared across the brand's own couriers, same zone
ndr_open_count
How many non-delivery reports are open right now		count of NDR records with status open, grouped by hours remaining in the attempt windowNDR queue tablelive
no courier NDR feed connected		not applicable, an operational queue count
cod_remittance_variance
Difference between what a courier should pay in COD and what it actually paid		sum of expected COD amount per order − sum of amount actually remitted, by courier and periodCOD remittance table joined to order recordsmonthly, per remittance cycle
remittance file not yet received from courier		not applicable; any nonzero variance is a dispute candidate
dead_stock_value
Value of stock that is old and barely moving		units aged past the brand's dead-stock threshold × landed_cost_per_unit, summed by SKUinventory age table joined to landed cost tablelive, weekly refresh
no inventory age data for that SKU		no external benchmark; threshold set per brand and category
price_lock_days_remaining
Days left before a supplier's price lock or contract expires		contract renewal_date − today, in dayssupplier contracts tablelive
no contract on file for that supplier		not applicable
three_way_match_exception_count
How many purchase orders have a quantity or price mismatch against the invoice and goods received		count of PO-invoice-GRN triplets where quantity or unit price differs beyond a set tolerancethree-way match tablelive, per PO closed
goods receipt not yet logged for that PO		not applicable, an exception count
commodity_input_cost_index
How the priced-in raw material behind a SKU is trending		current commodity price ÷ price on the date the SKU's cost was last locked, indexed to 100commodity price tracking tablemonthly
no tracked commodity mapped to that SKU		no external benchmark; used to time a cost-down ask
peak_season_readiness_pct
How ready stock, staffing and systems are for the next festive peak		average of (safety stock vs peak target, warehouse staffing booked vs target, WMS/OMS load-test pass) as percentagespeak season plan tablechecked weekly in the 90 days before the festival date
no peak season plan created for the upcoming festival		100% readiness target set per brand ahead of the date
ndr_to_rto_conversion_rate
Share of NDRs that end up going back as RTO instead of delivering		orders that end in RTO after an NDR ÷ total orders that had at least one NDR, trailing periodNDR queue table joined to shipment outcometrailing 30 days
fewer than 20 NDR cases in the window		compared against the brand's own trailing average, not an external figure

How it is drawn

  • Buy Box ownership timeline stacked area chart: share of time the Buy Box is held by the brand versus each tracked reseller, over the trailing 30 days
  • true versus reported ROAS bars grouped bar chart: quick_commerce_reported_roas next to quick_commerce_true_roas, per platform per period
  • listing health scorecard grid matrix grid: each channel down the side, each check (image, attribute, price match) across the top, colour-coded pass or fail
  • dark store fill rate map heat map: fill rate by store or region for each hero SKU
  • account health gauge gauge chart: each channel's account health score against its own warning and suspension thresholds
  • settlement reconciliation waterfall waterfall chart: expected settlement value stepping down through platform commission, fees and deductions to the amount actually received

Not in the usual tools

  • recomputes quick commerce ROAS from real transaction value instead of MRP, and shows the gap against the platform's own reported number
    vs Blinkit, Zepto and Instamart seller dashboards commonly report ROAS on MRP, which can overstate the real return by 30 to 50 percent
  • watches Buy Box, reviews, listing health and account risk together across Amazon, Flipkart, Myntra, Nykaa, Blinkit and Zepto in one room
    vs Amazon Brand Analytics only covers Amazon; each other marketplace requires its own seller portal, so no tool shows all of them side by side today
  • reconciles marketplace settlement payouts against order records automatically and routes a shortfall to Finance
    vs settlement checking is normally a manual tally against each platform's own downloaded report, done separately by finance
  • keeps one clean catalogue feed that Media buyer and Organic both pull from, with a staleness check
    vs self-serve ad panels like Blinkit's Brand Central manage that platform's own catalogue only, not a feed shared across channels and ad teams
  • splits an Amazon sales change into traffic versus conversion before anyone calls a product a failure
    vs Amazon's own Business Reports show the two numbers separately; this room does the split and states which one explains most of the change

  • P&L ladder waterfall chart: gross revenue stepping down through COGS, delivery cost, marketing cost and OPEX to net profit
  • 13-week cash forecast line chart with a marked floor: projected daily cash balance with the lowest point and its date flagged
  • contribution margin by SKU and channel ranked bar table: CM1, CM2, CM3 per SKU and per channel, sorted worst to best
  • CAC and payback trend line chart with a shaded LTGP:CAC band: blended CAC and payback months over time against the brand's own target band
  • platform-to-books bridge bridge/waterfall chart: the 5 recurring gaps (Shopify, Meta, GA4, processor, accounting) and their named causes
  • COD remittance aging bar chart by courier, bucketed by days late: how much COD cash is owed by each courier and how overdue it is
  • budget vs actual bar chart by OPEX category: budgeted vs actual spend per category, this month and trailing

Not in the usual tools

  • one P&L ladder from gross revenue to net profit with CM1, CM2 and CM3 labeled by scope, computed automatically from the brand's own order and cost data
    vs Triple Whale computes SKU-level contribution margin on its Advanced tier, but operating costs and a full P&L still go back to a spreadsheet
  • a rolling 13-week cash forecast tied to the same order, cost and payroll data as the P&L
    vs Shopify's native reports show revenue and basic cost, not contribution margin or a cash forecast; that work is done in a separate spreadsheet today
  • a promo break-even gate that blocks or clears a promo before it goes public, using the brand's real CM3
    vs generic discount-code apps report what a promo cost after the fact; they do not run a go/no-go check before launch
  • financing options stress-tested live against the brand's actual rolling cash forecast, not a one-time spreadsheet comparison
    vs financing marketplaces and lenders quote rates but do not simulate a specific structure against the brand's own forecast
  • COD cash tracked to the bank and escalated to the courier automatically once a remittance passes that courier's own SLA
    vs also in CODReconcile and Shiplystic for the collected-vs-remitted tracking; the drafted, SLA-cited escalation email is not part of those tools
  • all 5 recurring platform-to-books gaps reconciled in one place with a named cause each month
    vs today this is done manually across separate Shopify, Meta, GA4, processor and accounting exports

mer_and_amer

any of the 5 sources has not closed the month		target under 1% variance per gap
cod_remittance_gap

a courier has not sent its collection report		target: fully remitted within the courier's stated SLA

category not mapped in the budget		flag anything over 10% off budget
subscription_churn_rate

How it is drawn

  • audit scorecard traffic-light grid: GA4, Meta CAPI and tag-management checks, pass/warn/fail per check
  • attribution comparison grouped bar chart per channel: last-click, data-driven and platform-reported revenue side by side
  • funnel with baseline band step/funnel chart with a shaded baseline range: each funnel step's conversion against its own trailing 90-day band
  • data freshness heatmap heatmap, source by day: how stale each connected source is right now
  • RFM segment migration flow diagram between segment boxes: how many customers moved between RFM segments this period
  • daily trust strip row of pass/warn/fail dots over time: the daily trust score for the last 30 days

Not in the usual tools

  • GA4 config and event audit built into the same room as everything else that reads the brand's numbers
    vs also in Elevar, GA4 Auditor and Data Bloo for the audit itself; those tools do not connect it to Meta, Shopify or the metric contract
  • attribution models reconciled against each other and against a metric contract with one owner and freshness stamp per number
    vs Triple Whale (with Moby MMM) and Northbeam both reconcile click attribution with modeling, but neither ties the result to a written metric contract or flags when two dashboards define the same number differently
  • a daily pass/warn/fail trust score computed before anyone reads yesterday's numbers
    vs GA4 and Meta audit tools run on demand or on a schedule set by the vendor; this runs every day, before the numbers are read, and logs every setting change
  • GA4/Meta/Shopify/marketplace order counts reconciled and bot spikes flagged in the same view as spend and attribution
    vs Cloudflare's bot analysis tab shows bot score and traffic patterns on its own; it is not tied to order-count reconciliation or ad spend anomalies
  • one metric contract with a single formula, owner and freshness stamp per number, surfaced everywhere that number appears
    vs platforms report their own version of a number by their own definition (for example, one popular platform's built-in MER is spend÷revenue while the more common convention is revenue÷spend), and nothing today catches that mismatch automatically

How it is drawn

  • SOP coverage grid grid, role by status: every core role and whether its SOP is current, aging or missing
  • vendor SLA scorecard table with colored bars per vendor: each vendor's SLA metrics hit vs missed this period
  • meeting cadence strip calendar heat strip: which scheduled meetings actually happened over the last quarter
  • hiring trigger timeline line chart with a threshold band: the trigger metric for each silo against its threshold, with the crossing date marked
  • CS cost and time trend dual line chart: cost per ticket and resolution time over time, by complexity tier
  • decision log timeline timeline of logged decisions: every logged high-stakes decision with its owner, metric and review date

Not in the usual tools

  • SOP coverage tracked against the brand's own role list, with age and gaps flagged automatically
    vs wikis and doc tools (Notion, Confluence) store SOPs but do not flag which core role has none or which one is stale
  • vendor and 3PL SLA performance scored against the actual written SLA and cross-checked to invoices, in one room
    vs 3PL portals report their own operational numbers; matching those numbers to the signed SLA and the invoice is done manually today
  • a hiring trigger shown as a number crossed on a date, next to the org chart
    vs ATS tools track requisitions and pipeline once a search is open; they do not watch the metric that should open the search in the first place
  • SaaS renewal renegotiation drafted with the brand's own usage numbers before the auto-renew window closes
    vs SaaS spend-management tools (for example Vendr, Tropic) track contract dates and spend; they do not draft the renegotiation or cancellation email itself
  • CS cost per ticket and resolution time tied to a complexity definition set before routing, not after
    vs helpdesk tools (Gorgias, Zendesk) report cost and time per ticket; the complexity threshold that should decide routing is defined outside those tools today

Questions this room answers

  • what is hitting our site that is not a person, and what should happen to it
    build and enforce the bot and AI-agent traffic policy · MONEY-C41
  • are we losing money to card testing, false declines or return abuse
    detect fraud and abuse across checkout, cards and returns · MONEY-C42, MONEY-C76
  • could someone take over an account, and would we see it across every connected system
    harden account takeover and authentication defenses, correlated across systems · MONEY-C43, MONEY-C81
  • does our storefront or checkout code have an exploitable defect
    audit and fix web application security defects · MONEY-C44
  • what apps and scripts have access to our store, and are any of them risky
    audit third-party scripts, apps and dependencies · SITE-C13
  • does every staff member have only the access they need, and was it removed on time
    review admin access and least-privilege roles · MONEY-C46
  • if something breaks or gets ransomed today, can we actually recover
    maintain backups and the incident response runbook · MONEY-C47
  • is our domain safe, and is someone impersonating us
    monitor domain, DNS and brand-impersonation risk · MONEY-C48
  • are we exposed on GDPR/CCPA or consent rules for where our customers are
    audit privacy, consent and tracking compliance · MONEY-C49
  • are we covering all four security pillars, and which PCI questionnaire actually applies to us
    run the quarterly security audit program and confirm PCI scope · MONEY-C50, MONEY-C74
  • can someone spoof our domain in an email right now
    audit and enforce email authentication · MONEY-C72
  • which keys exist, who holds them, and which are overdue for rotation
    inventory and rotate API keys and credentials · MONEY-C73
  • would our own staff catch a phishing attempt today
    run staff security awareness training and phishing simulation · MONEY-C75
  • did this vendor pass a risk check before it touched customer or payment data
    run vendor security and data-processing risk assessments · MONEY-C77

How it is drawn

  • finding severity funnel stacked bar with age overlay: open findings by severity and how long each has been open
  • bot traffic trend line chart, checkout vs site-wide: bot traffic share over time, checkout endpoints separated from the rest of the site
  • admin access matrix heatmap, staff by admin area: who has access to what, and whether 2FA is on
  • backup and restore calendar calendar strip: every backup run and the last successful restore test date
  • credential rotation age table table, heat-colored by age: every API key or token and days since last rotation
  • domain and DNS risk list list with severity tags: dangling DNS records and new lookalike domains found this period

Not in the usual tools

  • open findings from the weekly scan tracked with age against a fix window and a named owner, in the same room as everything else
    vs also in Detectify, Qualys and similar scanners for finding the issue; those tools do not track it against the brand's own backlog and owner
  • bot traffic separated for checkout endpoints and tied to fraud and order-count anomalies
    vs also in Cloudflare's own bot analysis tab for the traffic pattern; it does not connect that traffic to chargeback risk or order-count reconciliation
  • backup health shown as a tested restore date, not just a backup existing
    vs backup providers report that a backup ran; whether it actually restores is a separate, often-skipped test that most brands do not track on a calendar
  • domain, DNS and lookalike-domain risk watched together with the brand's own registrar lock state
    vs domain monitoring services flag lookalikes; they do not also check the brand's own DNS for dangling records or registrar lock in the same view
  • credential rotation tracked with an overdue flag against a set schedule
    vs secrets vaults (for example a password manager or cloud secrets manager) store keys; they do not by default flag which ones are overdue for rotation against a brand-set schedule
  • account-takeover signals correlated across Shopify admin, ad accounts, the ESP and the payment processor in one place
    vs each platform has its own login and access logs; no single connected tool compares login geography and device across all of them to catch a pattern that only shows up together

With nothing connected, the room shows every avatar as provisional, every metric as unavailable, and an empty language bank. It tells the founder the fastest way in is a review export and a support ticket export, since those alone unlock avatar mining, the language bank, objection pairing and the first brand health line. Once reviews and orders land, the room fills in avatar evidence, repeat rate and review rating first, since those need no separate connector.

How it is drawn

  • avatar evidence board card grid, one card per avatar: job statement, 5W profile, quote count, freshness, provisional flag
  • language bank frequency chart horizontal bar list: top phrases by mention count, tagged used or unused in live copy
  • voice compliance heatmap heatmap, channel by period: pass or fail per audit for site, ads and email over time
  • brand health trend lines 4 small line charts: branded search, mention volume, net sentiment and repeat rate over time
  • objection to remover table paired table: each named objection next to its proven remover and coverage flag
  • mystery shopper journey funnel step funnel with pass or fail marks: browse, cart, checkout, confirmation, support reply, each marked pass or fail on the latest run

Not in the usual tools

  • the avatar record gates every field against a completeness checklist and marks itself provisional until a pattern shows across independent sources
    vs voice-of-customer tools like Mopinion or CustomerHero summarize themes but do not gate a single, traceable avatar record before it can be used
  • the language bank tracks reuse rate of real customer phrases in live copy, not just a tone score on a draft
    vs brand voice checkers like ClickUp, Averi and Sprites score whether a document sounds on-brand, but do not trace phrases back to a customer quote or measure whether the brand's own banked words ever get used
  • the consistency audit walks the live site, live ads and live email together on one cadence with one rule set
    vs voice tools check one document you paste in at a time, not the brand's whole live surface
  • brand health here joins branded search, mentions, sentiment and repeat purchase from the brand's own store data into one line
    vs brand tracking suites such as Brand24, Talkwalker or Brandwatch price from about 200 to 800 dollars a month and do not tie mention and sentiment data to the brand's own repeat-purchase numbers
  • the perception gap check compares the positioning statement word for word against the brand's own reviews and support tickets
    vs brand tracking tools survey a panel; this compares against what this brand's actual customers already said, for free
  • the mystery shopper audit runs the brand's own live journey end to end, including a real support query, on demand and repeatably
    vs a paid human mystery-shopper audit is a one-off snapshot, not a repeatable automated check
  • objection pairing here is the real thing that removed the objection for a real customer, tied to a support macro
    vs no off-the-shelf tool builds this pairing from the brand's own support and review evidence

How it is drawn

  • XY positioning map scatter plot, 2 axes: brand and every tracked competitor plotted, with open quadrants marked
  • price ladder horizontal band chart: brand and the 7 competitors' prices on a matched product, with the category range shaded
  • competitor change feed reverse-chronological timeline: new creative, price move or launch per competitor, most recent first
  • category ladder diagram vertical ladder: every rung from doing nothing up to the closest direct rival, with the brand's position marked
  • battlecard table comparison table: brand versus one named competitor, verified differences only, with a freshness flag
  • win-loss reason breakdown bar chart: count of losses to a named competitor, grouped by coded reason

Not in the usual tools

  • the positioning map plots the brand against every real shopper alternative, including doing nothing and adjacent categories, not only direct rivals
    vs Priceva and Prisync track prices across whatever sites you point them at, but do not build a positioning map of open ground
  • the price audit ties price band to the category's own learned range and the brand's premium signals, not just a raw price diff
    vs Prisync, Priceva and PriceIntelGuru report competitor prices and MAP violations but do not judge whether a price fits a premium position
  • competitor watch here reads only public signal (ads, sites, prices, launches) for the 7 brands already in this brand's gallery, and alerts only on real crossings
    vs Foreplay and PowerAdSpy alert on any new competitor creative across the whole market but do not tie it to this brand's own price position or open map quadrants
  • win-loss research asks real almost-buyers why they chose a named competitor, producing comparison language no price or ad tool can give
    vs no pricing or ad-spy tool interviews actual lost customers
  • the category ladder explicitly includes doing nothing and job-based alternatives outside the obvious category
    vs pricing and ad-monitoring tools only track named competitors already selling a similar product
  • battlecards here are gated on verified differences with a refresh cadence and a staleness flag
    vs a static competitor comparison page has no freshness check built in

Brand · BRAND

Wild bets and PR

Used by founder, brand manager, PR contact, agency account manager. 14 jobs, 15 metrics, 9 insights computed in code, 16 actions.

What the room would say insights are rules, not opinions
{bet} has sat in {stage} for {n} days, past its own plan.
rule: bet_cycle_time_days exceeds the bet's own planned duration for its current stage
Approve the bound and move to executingNot nowBRAND-C45
{bet} showed no real lift over the control group at its read point.
rule: bet_read_point_lift is at or below zero against control at the bet's own read point date
Pull the read point resultNot nowREG-C02
{bet} beat its control by {n}. Worth sizing up.
rule: bet_read_point_lift is positive and above the bet's own pre-set bar at its read point
Pull the read point resultNot nowREG-C02
Mentions of the brand jumped {x}x this week with a negative tilt. A holding statement is drafted and waiting.
rule: mention or review volume in the reputation monitor rises more than 2x the trailing average with a negative tilt
Send the holding statement for approvalNot nowBRAND-C54
{n} contacts on the {story} list need a re-check before this pitch goes out.
rule: a contact on the current media list has not been reachable or has changed beat since the list was built
Build or refresh the media listNot nowBRAND-C53
{moment} is {n} days away with no brief started.
rule: a calendar moment falls within 30 days and has no linked brief
Update the festive and cultural calendarNot nowLOOP-C05

With nothing connected, the bet pipeline board starts empty except for whatever ideas are logged by hand, the PR timeline and media list show no entries, and the reputation monitor stays unavailable until a mention feed is attached. The first useful state comes from logging even one bet with its 8-field bound, since that alone lights up the pipeline board, the bound-completeness metric and the read-point chart once the read date arrives.

Questions this room answers

  • what unconventional moves are worth considering
    keep a dated backlog of wild bet candidates · BRAND-C44
  • what is the downside, and when do we call it
    bound and stage-gate a bet before it launches · BRAND-C45
  • how do we actually run this at a size we can handle
    turn an approved bet into a sized execution brief · BRAND-C46
  • did this bet actually work
    read a bet at its read point against a real control · REG-C02
  • who counts as 'us', and how do we run them day to day
    design and run an exclusive cohort or ambassador program · BRAND-C39, BRAND-C55
  • why would someone actually tell a friend about us
    design referral and word-of-mouth mechanics with a real reason to tell a friend · LIFE-C45
  • who should we team up with, and who approached us that we should screen
    source and screen partnership candidates for fit · BRAND-C41, BRAND-C70
  • could this person or brand embarrass us later
    check reputational risk before signing a partner, licensee or ambassador · BRAND-C71
  • what is the story here that a journalist would actually run
    develop PR angles and press narratives · BRAND-C40
  • who exactly should get this pitch
    build a targeted media list for a launch or story · BRAND-C53
  • is a problem building that we need to get ahead of
    monitor for reputation risk and prepare a holding statement · BRAND-C54
  • where should the founder show up, and what are we eligible to win
    build the founder's public credibility pipeline · BRAND-C60, BRAND-C61
  • what dates matter for PR, events and community this quarter
    build the brand's cultural and festive moment calendar · LOOP-C05
  • where does our exact customer already gather, and can we show up there
    design an event or pop-up sized to what the team can run · BRAND-C42

How it is drawn

  • bet pipeline board kanban, one column per stage: every wild bet from idea to bounded to executing to read point to scaled or killed
  • read point comparison chart bar pair per bet: treatment versus control on the bet's own named metric
  • PR coverage timeline reverse-chronological timeline: placements landed, outlet, and reach where available
  • festive and cultural calendar calendar view, 90-day rolling window: upcoming dates with brief-ready status color coded
  • partnership funnel funnel chart: candidates sourced, screened, reputation-checked, approved
  • reputation risk monitor line chart with alert markers: mention or review volume over time with spikes marked

Not in the usual tools

  • the bet pipeline enforces an 8-field bound (budget cap, downside limit, control design, cohort, stop rule, read point, legal review, and the bound itself) before a bet can launch, and reads it against a real control in code
    vs no off-the-shelf PR or project management tool gates a marketing bet against a written downside limit and a measured control group
  • the reputation monitor ties a mention or review spike directly to a drafted holding statement in the same room
    vs Muck Rack and Prowly monitor coverage and mentions but do not draft a holding statement tied to this brand's own crisis playbook
  • partnership sourcing and reputation screening run in one pipeline, checked against the brand's own non-competitive, same-customer rule
    vs generic partnership or CRM tools track deals but do not screen for category overlap or run a reputation check as one gated step
  • the media list is built and verified per specific story or launch, matched to real beats
    vs Muck Rack and Prowly sell a static, purchased journalist database; this room builds a small verified list for the story at hand
  • the festive and cultural calendar is scoped to this brand's own category, PR needs, events and community moments, not a generic marketing holiday calendar
    vs generic marketing calendars list every holiday, not the ones that matter for this brand's category and PR angles
  • the ambassador program room runs screening, task assignment, content approval and tracking end to end for this brand's own program
    vs generic UGC rights tools manage content rights but do not run the day-to-day of an approved ambassador program

Data layer

The tables the rooms need, by source.

Union of every data need across the rooms. Tables used by several rooms come first in each group and are the ones to build first. Every table carries a brand key, a captured or computed stamp and a source. Windows end on the last full day. Missing stays null and shows as unavailable.

Design rules

What makes a room actionable.

Metrics

Every metric has a formula, a source table, a window and an unavailable rule. Computed in code. A founder can click any number and see how it was made.

Insights

An insight is a rule code can run, and the sentence it produces: what we saw, the size, the cause. No insight needs the model's judgement. Ranked by money or time saved.

Actions

Each insight carries the buttons for its capability: let the agent do it, send to a person, not now. The autonomy level comes from the catalogue. Decisions go to the registry with a read point.

Visuals

The chart fits the question: a fix chain for speed, a bridge for margin, a cohort grid for repeat, a heatmap for freshness. Charts the lab already proved (the filmstrip, the before-and-after chain, ad-level gallery) are marked as existing.

Not elsewhere

Each room names what the usual tools do not show, and says "also in X" where a tool already does it. The common thread: joining the brand's own orders to every channel, and measured before-and-after instead of scores.

Empty state

Before data connects, a room shows unavailable, says which connector fixes it, and offers the first action that needs no data (a baseline run, a crawl, an interview). The first insight appears the moment the first table lands.
